Food prepared with excessive salt, spices, or vinegar causes burning sensation (विदाही/burning feeling) and vitiates pitta and blood (पित्त रक्त दूषण/pitta blood vitiator).
Food cooked with ghee and milk (घृत क्षीर पक्व/ghee milk cooked food) is wholesome (पथ्य/wholesome), nourishing (बृंहण/nourishing), and aphrodisiac (वृष्य/virility booster).
Food prepared by deep frying in oil (तैल भृष्ट/oil fried food) is heavy to digest (गुरु/heavy digestion), hot in potency (उष्ण/heating), and causes skin diseases (कुष्ठकर/skin disease causing).
Roasted meats cooked on charcoal (अंगार पक्व मांस/charcoal roasted meat) are light to digest (लघु/light digestion) and appetizing (रुचिकर/taste booster).
Meats cooked in ovens or earthen pots (पिठर पक्व/pot roasted meat) are unctuous (स्निग्ध/unctuous) and provide strength (बल्य/strength giving).
Food cooked with sour fruits and curds (अम्ल सिद्ध/sour cooked food) increases pitta and kapha (पित्त कफकर/pitta kapha increaser).
Food items that are sweet, unctuous, and heavy should be eaten first in meal (प्रथमाशन/first part of meal).
Items that are sour and salty should be consumed in middle of meal (मध्याशन/middle of meal).
Items that are pungent, bitter, and astringent should be eaten at end of meal (अंताशन/last part of meal).
Fruits like pomegranate and sugarcane should be consumed in beginning of meal (भोजनादौ/before meal).
Consuming food in proper order maintains health (स्वास्थ्य रक्षण/health preservation) and keeps humors balanced (दोष साम्य/humor balance).
Overeating (अत्यशन/overeating) destroys digestive fire (अग्निनाशन/digestion destroyer) and causes indigestion diseases (अजीर्ण रोग/indigestion illness).
Eating before previous meal is digested (अध्यशन/eating over undigested food) causes severe toxic accumulation (विषम आम/severe toxic residue).
Eating proper quantity according to individual digestive power (मात्रावत् अश्रन/measured eating) promotes longevity (आयुर्वर्धन/longevity promoter).
One third of stomach should be filled with solid food (एक तृतीय भाग ठोस/one third solid).
One third of stomach should be filled with liquids (एक तृतीय भाग द्रव/one third liquid).
Remaining one third of stomach should be left empty for free movement of humors (एक तृतीय भाग वायु हेतु/one third empty for air).
Warm fresh food (उष्ण स्निग्ध अन्न/warm unctuous food) promotes digestion (सुपाच्य/easy digesting) and kindles digestive fire (दीपन/digestion booster).
Eating in pleasant clean environment with peaceful mind (प्रसन्न चित्त/peaceful mind) ensures proper assimilation of food.
Eating food very fast (द्रुत भुक्त/eating too fast) leads to wrong pathway entry and choking.
Eating food extremely slowly (अति विलंबित/eating too slow) causes coldness and uneven digestion.
Thus ends the classification of cooked food preparations (कृतान्नवर्ग/cooked foods group).
Meat properties (मांस गुण/meat qualities): provides nourishment (बृंहण/nourishing), increases strength (बलवर्धक/stamina booster), and pacifies vata humor (वातशामक/vata pacifier).

Thus ends the classification of spices, salts, and dietary auxiliaries (औषध लवण वर्ग/spices and salts group).
Rules of dietetics (मात्रा नियम/dietary regimen): eat wholesome food in measured quantity (मात्रावत् अशन/measured eating) to preserve health (स्वास्थ्य रक्षण/health protection).

Thus ends the seventh chapter (सप्तमः अध्यायः/seventh chapter) named 'Nature of Food Substances' (अन्नस्वरूपविज्ञानीयः/knowledge of food nature) of Ashtanga Sangraha.




Now, therefore, we shall explain the chapter (अध्याय/section) on the rules for food protection (अन्नरक्षा/food safety).
For rulers and wealthy lords (ईश्वर/rulers), especially kings (राजा/king), enemies (अमित्र/enemies) are generally more than friends.
Therefore, persons employed by enemies attempt to give poison (विष/poison) in food and drinks (अन्नपान/food and beverages).
Even royal women (स्त्री/women) employed by agents may do so out of desire for supreme favor (सौभाग्य/favor).
Therefore, the king should employ an expert physician (प्राणाचार्य/royal doctor) who is noble, faithful, devoted, righteous, skilled (दक्ष/capable), well-versed in Ayurvedic eight branches (अष्टांग/eight branches), and proficient in antidote formulations (अगद/antidote).
The king should honor him with wealth and respect (अर्थ मान/wealth and honor) as a disciple honors a teacher (गुरु/master) or a son honors a father (पिता/father).
Even if his advice feels unfavorable (प्रतिकूल/unpalatable), the king should regard it as timely and beneficial (हित/wholesome).
For even a noble elephant (गजपति/king elephant) without a control hook (अंकुश/goad) is not praised by people.
Therefore, the king should entrust his diet and daily routine (आहार विहार/diet and lifestyle) entirely to the physician.
Even an attained lifespan (जीवित/life span) stays protected within its time through proper medical measures (उपाय/remedy).
Furthermore (अपिच/moreover):
Kings have many responsibilities, and swift-acting diseases like colic and collapse (शूल संन्यास/colic and syncope) require constant medical monitoring.
Because kings are surrounded by distractions, intolerant of pain, prone to unwholesome desires, and encircled by flattering attendants (परिवार/entourage).
Therefore, the king should make the physician reside right near the royal palace (राजगृह/royal mansion).
So that (तथाहि/for instance):
He can directly supervise (अपरोक्ष/direct vision) all equipments and items used for the king's body.
The physician should stand before the king, holding protective herbs (औषधि/medicines), and serve thoroughly tested, pure food that was first offered to fire (अग्नि/fire) and inspected by peacocks (शिखि/peacock).
While the king is eating, drums smeared with anti-poison antidotes (अगद/antidote drums) should be sounded.
In poisoned food (सविष अन्न/poisoned food), liquid boiled food becomes non-dripping, cooks slowly, appears stale immediately, turns cold, loses normal color, and shows moon-like circular spots (चंद्रिका/circular spots).
Side dishes dry up quickly, broth turns dark/blackish, shadows appear distorted or invisible, and foam layers, thread-like lines, and bubbles (फेन बुद्बुद/foam and bubbles) appear on top.
Especially in salty preparations (लवण/salty food), a dense layer of foam (फेनमाला/foam ring) forms.
In meat soup (रस/meat broth), a blue line (नील राजी/blue streak) appears in the middle.
In milk (पयस्/milk), a copper-colored line (ताम्र/copper hue) appears.
In wine and water (मद्य तोय/alcohol and water), a black line (काली राजी/black streak) appears.
In curd (दधि/curd), a greyish line (श्यावा/grey streak) appears.
In buttermilk (तक्र/buttermilk), a bluish-yellow line (नील पीता/bluish yellow streak) appears.
In whey/liquid milk part (मस्तु/whey), a pigeon-grey color (कपोताभा/pigeon color) appears.
In fermented grain gruel (धान्याम्ल/sour gruel), a black color (कृष्णा/black) appears.
In liquid medicine (द्रव औषध/liquid drug), a brownish color (कपिला/brown hue) appears.
In ghee (घृत/clarified butter), a watery appearance (सलिलाभा/water-like look) appears.
In honey (क्षौद्र/honey), a greenish color (हरिता/greenish hue) appears.
In oil (तैल/oil), a reddish tint (अरुणा/reddish color) and fat smell (वसा गंध/fat odor) appear.
Unripe fruits (आम फल/unripe fruit) ripen prematurely when poisoned.
Ripe fruits (पक्व फल/ripe fruit) undergo rapid putrefaction and rotting (प्रकोथ/decay).
Wet/fresh substances (आर्द्र द्रव्य/moist items) suddenly wither (म्लानता/wilting) and lose their natural state.
Dry substances (शुष्क द्रव्य/dry items) turn dark, blackish, and discolored (वैवर्ण्य/discoloration).
Hard articles (कठिन द्रव्य/hard items) become soft (मृदु/soft).
Soft articles (मृदु द्रव्य/soft items) become hard (कठिन/hard).
Garlands (माल्य/flower garland) wither, lose fragrance (गंध नाश/loss of smell), and their flower tips burst open.
Bedspreads and garments (वस्त्र/clothing) show dark patches, threads separate, and hair fibers fall off.
Metallic and gem articles (लोह मणि/metals and gems) get coated with dirt, lose shine, weight, color, and smooth touch.
The poisoner (विषद/poison giver), terrified of his crime, shows sweating, trembling (वेपथु/shivering), dry dark face, and looks around anxiously (सोद्वेग/anxiously).
He looks with special anxiety toward the place where he introduced the poison.
His upper garment slips, he hides behind pillars or walls, walks falteringly, feels ashamed, answers incoherently when asked, stammers, cracks fingers, scratches head, licks lips, yawns, scrapes the ground, acts in haste, and cannot stand firm.
However, some innocent nervous persons (अनवस्थित/unsteady mind) may also act similarly due to fear of king's command or other faults.
Therefore, one must test food on fire (अग्नि/fire) and other means.
Fire coming in contact with poisoned food turns into a single swirl, produces dry dim flame, rainbow colors (इंद्रधनुष/rainbow hues), and loud crackling sounds (चटचटायते/crackles).
The smoke smells like a corpse (कुणप गंध/cadaver smell) and causes fainting, salivation, goosebumps, headache, rhinitis, and blurred vision (दृष्टि/eyesight trouble).
In such cases, one should apply nasal drops and eye salve (नस्य अंजन/nasal drops and collyrium) made of spikenard, saussurea, and cuscus grass with honey.
Or inhale smoke prepared from chaff-flower, vidanga, bala, chitrak, and grapes mixed with ghee and jaggery (घृत गुड/ghee and jaggery).
Fire may also show unusual flame due to fat or salt combinations.
Therefore, one should also test food using birds (वयांसि/birds).
Crows (काक/crow) eating poisoned food utter feeble harsh sounds (क्षाम स्वर/weak voice).
Flies (मक्षिका/houseflies) do not sit on poisoned food.
If flies sit on it, they die immediately (विपद्यंते/die).
Upon mere sight of poisoned food, the eyes of the Greek pheasant/Chakora bird (चकोर/chakora bird) turn red.
The voice of the cuckoo/Kokila (कोकिल/cuckoo) undergoes abnormal distortion.
The gait of the swan (हंस/swan) falters and stumbles.
The bhringaraja bird (भृंगराज/fork-tailed drongo) hums mournfully.
The demoiselle crane/Krouncha (क्रौंच/crane) becomes intoxicated.
The rooster/cock (कृकवाकु/rooster) crows loudly in distress.
The parrot (शुक/parrot) screams out aloud.
The maina bird (सारिका/starling maina) vomits (छर्दि/vomit).
The golden oriole (चामीकर/golden bird) flies away.
The duck/Karandava (कारंडव/water duck) dies on the spot.
The Jeevanjeevaka pheasant (जीवंजीवक/pheasant) faints and languishes.
The mongoose (नकुल/mongoose) gets erection of body hairs (हृष्ट रोमा/goosebumps).
The monkey (वानर/monkey) passes feces, the spotted deer (पृषत/spotted deer) cries, and the peacock (मयूर/peacock) becomes overjoyed; by peacock's mere sight, poison loses its intensity.
Vapor from poisoned food rises with a peacock-neck color (मयूर कंठ/peacock neck blue-green color).
Inhaling that vapor causes fainting and stupor (मूर्च्छा/fainting).
Treatment for that vapor inhalation is similar to poison smoke treatment.
Touching poisoned food with hands causes burning sensation, swelling, numbness, and falling of fingernails (नख शात/nail shedding).
For that, paste of indigo, cochineal insect, and blue water lily (श्याम इंद्रगोप उत्पल/indigo and water lily) should be applied.
Eating poisoned food causes tingling lips, burning inside mouth, heavy tongue root, jaw stiffness (हनुस्तंभ/jaw lock), tooth sensitivity, excessive salivation, and loss of taste perception.
In that condition, therapies prescribed for poison smoke and poisoned toothbrush should be performed.
When poison reaches the stomach (आमाशय/stomach), it causes sweating, intoxication, fainting, vomiting, discoloration, flatulence, shivering, burning, anorexia, and cardiac distress.
In that stage, emesis (वमन/vomiting) should be induced immediately using emetic nut, bottle gourd, and sponge gourd with curd and honey.
After proper emesis, when the body is oleated, purgation (विरेचन/purgation) should be administered.
Drink powder of triphala, trikatu, nagakeshara, liquorice, and peacock-feather ash mixed with bile (पित्त/bile) of lion, tiger, wolf, leopard, or iguana and honey; this is Jeevana Agada (जीवन अगद/life-restoring antidote), supreme cure for all poisons.
When that is digested, feed rice gruel cooked with trikatu, milk, and ghee (पयस् घृत/milk and ghee).
After gruel digestion, give light boiled rice with green gram soup cooked with trikatu and ghee.
Apply paste of liquorice, albizia, and sandalwood (चंदन/sandalwood) over the body.
When poison reaches the large intestine (पक्वाशय/large intestine), it causes thirst, burning, fainting, diarrhea, tympanites, sensory organ dysfunction, debility, emaciation, and anemia.
In that stage, administer purgation using ghee cooked with indigo fruit (नीलिनी/indigo).
Administer Dushi-vishari antidote (दूषीविषारि अगद/anti-latent-poison formulation) with honey and curd.
Using a poisoned toothbrush causes bristling brush fibers, drug odor, dryness, and swelling of tongue, teeth, lips, and oral gums.
Apply paste of dhátaki flowers, jamun seeds, and myrobalan with honey on gums.
Gargle (गंडूष/gargle) with juice of pomegranate, karamarda, mango, and plum mixed with honey.
By this, procedures for tongue scrapers, mouth gargles, and rinses are also explained.
Using poisoned eye salve causes tearing, discharge, burning, redness, pain, distorted vision, and blindness (आंध्य/blindness).
In that condition, drinking ghee (सर्पिः पान/ghee intake) is indicated.
Boil milk with long pepper and roasted grain flour.
Prepare ghee with that paste for soothing eye irrigation (नेत्र तर्पण/eye pooling).
Apply collyrium made of kapittha, meshashringi, and bhallataka flowers with varna resin.
Inhale or apply powder of brihati, albizia seeds, lotus, and nagabala steeped in honey.
Eye collyrium combined with antimony (स्त्रोतोंजन/antimony) and gold dust is beneficial.
Using poisoned nasal drops or snuff causes headache, phlegm discharge, nasal bleeding, and sensory impairment.
Administer ghee cooked with ativisha, kaakamachi, and madayantika in milk for nasal drops and drink.
Using poisoned massage oil causes skin burning, sweating, suppuration, eruptions, and skin cracking.
Sprinkle cold water and apply paste of sandalwood, ushir, kushta, lotus, and gulvel.
Drink the same formulation mixed with kapittha juice and cow urine (गोमूत्र/cow urine).
Apply paste of girikarnika, priyangu, sariva, liquorice, and bael root crushed in decanting water.
By this, rules for poisoned body pastes, powders, baths, ornaments, vehicles, beds, garments, armors, chariots, and footwear are explained.
Specifically for disorders caused by poisoned ornaments, apply paste of ashwagandha, apamarga, and khadir with cow bile (गोपित्त/ox bile).
For poisoned footstools, apply paste of shleshmataka and sarpasugandha with honey.
Using a poisoned umbrella causes rapid eruption and suppuration of blisters resembling ripe jamun berries.
Apply paste of liquorice, patala, kasheruka, lodhra, and khadir over the entire body.
By this, rules for poisoned royal fly-whisks and fans (चामर व्यजन/fly-whisk and fan) are also explained.
Using poisoned head massage oil causes head pain, skin nodules, and hair fall (केश च्यवन/hair fall).
Apply mud paste steeped in black nightshade, amaranth, ghee, and honey over the head.
Or apply cow dung juice, malati, soot from chimney (अगारधूम/kitchen soot), or wild rat dung paste.
Pour decoction of shleshmataka bark, patala, albizia, liquorice, and turmeric mixed with goat milk (अजा क्षीर/goat milk) over the head.
By this, rules for head baths, water, combs, garlands, and turbans (उष्णीष/turban) are explained.
Using poisoned ear drops causes ear swelling, earache, suppuration, and hearing loss (श्रोत्र वैगुण्य/deafness).
Fill ear with juice of bahuputra mixed with ghee and honey, or cold juice of somavalka.
Using poisoned face ointment causes facial dark spots, prickly skin eruptions, and massage oil symptoms.
Apply face pack of liquorice, milkweed, bandhujiva, and sandalwood with ghee, and drink ghee.
Smelling poisoned flowers causes headache, tearing eyes, and loss of smell sensation (गंध अज्ञान/anosmia).
In that condition, apply procedures mentioned above for smoke and vapor inhalation.
Here are verse stanzas (भवति चात्र/here are verses):
The wash-water of fruits, roots, and leaves (फल मूल प्रक्षालन/washing water) should be tested.
Similarly, the physician should carefully check vessels and side dishes without laxity.
First smell auspicious items, then touch them gently.
Add antidotes (प्रतिवाप/antidote addition) and wait for one nadika measure (नाडिका/24 minutes).
After verifying the purity of both vessel and water (भाजन उदक/vessel and water),
The king (वसुधाधिप/ruler of earth) should partake of his food appropriately.
Mild poison loses its potency when brought near strong anti-poison charms.
Therefore, a wise physician should tie potent anti-poison drugs (विषघ्न/anti-poison) on his hand.
Holding anti-poison charms bestows fortune, destroys evil spirits, and increases affection.
Furthermore (अपिच/moreover):
Medicine storehouse containing pots, crucibles, and drug shelves,
Should be located in the east or north direction (प्राक् उदीची/east or north direction).
It should be built high, in an auspicious spot, with many windows (वातायन/windows), and spacious.
The royal kitchen (महानस/kitchen) must be well cleaned and served by trustworthy staff.
Its entrance should be guarded by sentries, with proper ceiling and canopy.
It should have clean water pots, well-washed vessels, pure water, and clean fuel.
Cooks (सूद/cooks) working there should be skilled, prompt, vigilant, and honest.
They should have trimmed hair and nails, hereditary loyalty to king, and no external ties.
Their supervisor should be a noble brahmin, well-tested, disciplined, and clean.
He should be devoted, loyal, pure, and obedient to the royal physician.
All attendants staying near the king should always possess such noble virtues.
Free from mutual conflicts and petty factions, they must work solely for king's welfare.
The single virtuous physician should mentally watch over all of them vigilantly.
Constantly devoted to protecting the king's house, diet, and equipments.
When the king marches against enemies, the physician equipped with medicines, instruments, and surgical tools (भेषज शस्त्र यंत्र/drugs, tools, and instruments),
Should pitch his tent near the royal flag and treat wounded soldiers in war (युद्ध/battlefield).
Because enemies corrupt the paths, water, shade, food, fodder, and firewood (मार्ग उदक अन्न/paths, water, and food),
The physician should identify all poisoned items and purify them immediately.
Never permit marching or military camping without inspecting the route.
Soil, water, grass, timber, stones, routes, and roadside trees,
When contaminated with poison, look smeared and abnormal.
Grass, shrubs, and plants wither, and insects and reptiles die.
Animals walking on poisoned ground suffer hooves and nails falling off, burning, itching, and pain.
Troops suffer vomiting, fainting, fever, confusion, and severe headache.
For ground decontamination, drink drumstick root, somavalli, ushir, citrus juice, and asafoetida (हिंगु/asafoetida).
Administer the remedy in proper dose according to constitution.
Mix urine of goats, sheep, and elephants with animal blood and meat,
Combine with all aromatic spices (सर्वगंध/all aromatics), boil, and sprinkle over poisoned ground.
Mix babchi, sunanda, sarala, gandhanakuli, charati, and trayamana,
And sprinkle that liquid over the poisoned terrain to purify it.
Poisoned water becomes tasteless, lukewarm, marked with strange streaks,
Foamy, heavy, separated, avoided by birds, and full of dead fish.
Touching poisoned water causes pain, inflammation, vesicles, and severe itching.
Rice cooked in poisoned water causes burning sensation immediately upon eating.
It digests with great difficulty; if uncooked, it causes fainting and fever.
Poisoned food shows blue, yellow, red, and multi-colored patches.
In such cases, sprinkle and drink Shigru-agada (शिग्वादि अगद/drumstick antidote formulation) to cure ground poison.
Burn ajashringi, vishala, vishaghni, uttamaroni, phanijjaka, and ativisha into ash.
Filter the ash multiple times through cloth,
Boil the filtrate and add paste of sarala and turmeric,
Along with cardamom, ushir, manjishtha, sunanda, and babchi.
Pour drops of this liquid into water; wherever it touches, water becomes poison-free.
Burn patala, paribhadra, ashvakarna, shamyaka, and shigru inside a clay pot,
And throw the ash into poisoned water reservoirs.
In cold season, poisoned wind feels hot; in summer, it feels icy cold.
Poisoned air causes clouds, fainting, and respiratory distress.
In that condition, Shigru-agada antidote is recommended.
Powder devdaru, nata, anantamula, liquorice, antimony, red ochre (गैरिक/red ochre),
Vajrakanda, lata, and lodhra into fine dust.
Dust this powder on tree tops, royal flags, and high banners.
Upon contact with this fine powder, the air becomes completely purified and poison-free.
When a tree is poisoned, its shade turns abnormal and distorted.
Its flowers become odorless or foul-smelling, causing headache and cardiac distress.
Its fruits and leaves cause itching, skin suppuration, and severe diarrhea.
All remedies prescribed for soil poison are effective for treating poisoned trees.
Never touch or approach an unknown, unexamined maiden (कन्या/maiden).
For clever malicious people prepare various sinister poison combinations.
A poison maiden (विषकन्या/poison maiden) is fed minute poison doses from birth.
Her mere touch, breath, or sweat kills a person; her test is as follows:
By the touch of her sweat or breath, flower petals wilt immediately.
Bedbugs, lice, and water organisms die instantly in her bed or bathwater.
Knowing such signs, one should avoid a poison maiden from afar.
The king should never consume anything uninspected, unblessed by physician, or untested by cooks.
Food must first be tasted by royal cooks and tested before serving the king.
The auspicious eye collyrium named Sarvarthasiddhi (सर्वार्थसिद्धि अगद/all-accomplishing antidote salve) destroys sins, evil spirits, and poisons.
It is supreme for eyesight, longevity, destroying enemies, and granting royal victory.
During bright fortnight on auspicious nakshatra like Pushya or Punarvasu, gather pure blue antimony (अंजन/antimony collyrium) from Indus river bed.
Mix eight parts antimony with one part gold dust, silver, and copper in a crucible, offering prayers and firing with khadir wood.
Worship Avalokiteshvara, Tara, Brahma, Ashvins, Indra, Agni, Dhanvantari, and Sushruta with fried grain, jaggery, ghee, and milk rice; quench the heated collyrium separately in cow dung, urine, ghee, milk, curd, honey, fat, marrow, wine, and herbal decoctions.
Tie it in white silk cloth and suspend it in rainwater for twelve days.
Dry in shade, grind with pearls, crystals, and gold dust through a pure maiden, and store in a gold, silver, or gem collyrium pot.
Consecrate it with Savitri sacred mantras and Vedic rituals.
Carry it on an elephant back with white umbrella, fly-whisks, conch shells, and Vedic chants to the royal physician's home.
The physician, purified after worshipping cows and brahmins, should apply this divine eye salve using a gold rod while reciting the holy Dharani incantation.
Homage to the King of Eye Purification, the Tathagata, Arhat, Perfectly Awakened Buddha!
Namely (तद्यथा/namely):
Om! Purify my physical eye, wisdom eye, knowledge eye, and spiritual vision! Svaha!
Thereafter, remembering this sacred Dharani every morning and evening,
Apply this supreme, holy, health-giving collyrium originally created by Ashvin twins for Indra.
Therefore, a wise physician should prepare this for kings, ministers, and scholars.
Here are additional protective stanzas (भवंति चात्र/here are verses):
Now auspicious formulations declared by Lord Brihaspati (बृहस्पति/guru Brihaspati) will be stated,
By using which a king is never deceived or harmed by enemies.
Decoction of bael, mahua, barley ash, patala, and shalmali,
Is a supreme anti-poison sprinkling wash (प्रोक्षण/sprinkling wash).
Sprinkling poisoned items with it renders them instantly poison-free.
For fodder, fuel, water, clothes, beds, seats, cooked rice,
Armors, ornaments, umbrellas, fans, and royal residences.
Shleshmataka, patala, ativisha, drumstick, sariva, punarnava,
Samanga, poison root bark, kapittha, and bull's blood,
Mixed with pomegranate peel forms a supreme anti-poison wash.
Lac, priyangu, manjishtha, samanga, reuka, liquorice,
Ground with honey and brown mongoose bile (बभ्रु पित्त/mongoose bile),
Kept inside a cow horn buried underground for seven nights.
Made into a protective gem amulet encased in gold and worn on hand.
Whatever poisoned item touches this gem becomes immediately poison-free.
Realgar, orpiment, shami flowers, white mustard, kapittha, kushta, manjishtha,
Ground finely with bile of dog, cow, and brown cat.
This supreme antidote named Soumya (सौम्य अगद/soumya antidote) should be worn as a gem.
It acts as a supreme poison conqueror when tied on hand.
Or roots of musakaja tied on hand destroy all poisons.
Harenu, jatamansi, manjishtha, turmeric, liquorice, honey,
Terminalia bark, holy basil, lac, and dog bile made into a gem amulet.
Musical instruments and royal banners smeared with this paste,
Render anyone hearing, seeing, or smelling them instantly free from poison.
Trikatu, five salts (पंचलवण/five medicinal salts), manjishtha, two turmerics,
Cardamom, trivrit, tejpatta, vidanga, and indravaruni,
Mixed with liquorice and honey, kept inside a cow horn (गोविषाण/cow horn).
Taken in proper dose with warm water before meals,
Causes ingested poison to digest without causing any harm to the body.
Fumigation (धूप/incense fumigation) with lac, sal resin, ushir, mustard, cardamom, bhallataka, and arjuna flowers,
In the bedroom destroys all stationary and mobile poisons.
No venomous insects, rats, or reptiles can stay where this fumigation burns.
Nor can evil sorcery or witch spells affect that home.
Fumigation with peacock feathers, crane bones, mustard, sandalwood, and ghee,
Purifies beds, clothes, seats, and royal mansions from poison.
Vishala, trikatu, manjishtha, liquorice, five salts, turmerics, trivrit,
Mixed with honey and stored in cow horn, added to bath water.
Decoction of sun-flower, white apamarga, and mustard with curd and ghee,
Or paste of sandalwood, milk, and dhátaki bark,
Purifies all water sources and bath waters.
Applying facial tilak mark made of rochana, lotus, and saffron,
Protects against poison and makes one beloved to royal women.
Dusting powders of turmeric, manjishtha, and neem renders items poison-free, says Sage Gautama.
Administer life-restoring antidotes through nasal drops, drinks, collyrium, and pastes.
Use sharp emetics with long pepper, liquorice, honey, sugar, and sugarcane juice.
Perform purgation and bloodletting (विरेचन सिरामोक्ष/purgation and venesection) when indicated.
First protect the cardiac region (हृदयावरण/heart protection) before administering anti-poison cures.
Drink Ajeya Ghrita (अजेय घृत/unconquerable ghee antidote) on an empty stomach.
Ghee, honey, curd, milk, cold water, and meat soup are beneficial.
Meat soup of iguana, peacock, mongoose, and deer cooked with ginger and long pepper.
Served cool with ghee to a person who has consumed poison.
After proper emesis and purgation, administer fine copper dust (ताम्र रजः/copper powder) with honey to purify the heart.
Once the heart is purified, administer gold powder (हेम चूर्ण/gold dust/swarna bhasma).
Poison cannot cling to a person protected by gold, just as water drops do not stick to a lotus leaf.
He attains long life and vitality; this same procedure applies for artificial poisons (गरविष/artificial toxin).
Thus the physician must protect the king from poison and artificial toxins.
For the downfall of a king brings destruction to all subjects and their undertakings.
Command, courage, patience, and generosity in a king are superhuman divine qualities.
Therefore, the king should be served with deep devotion like a deity.
The physician should enter the royal chamber only with permission and recognition.
Even if favored, he should never occupy improper seats or vehicles.
He should stand humbly before the king and never interrupt his speech.
Speak timely, righteous words concerning the king's interest and public welfare.
Never advise the king unasked, for that is considered great rashness.
Never suggest unpalatable measures that cause radical harm.
Speak agreeable and beneficial words; if harmful, warn him in private.
Use gentle soothing words; if he overlooks faults, remain silent.
Avoid hostile debates or offensive conversations in royal presence.
A scholar who ignores the king's mood fails, while a wise attendant thrives.
Excessive intimacy or hostility with a king can quickly turn disastrous.
Inform the king before undertaking even small tasks.
Do not linger unnecessarily in the royal treasury or inner quarters.
Express contentment with even small gains, without arrogance.
Avoid secret whispering, gossiping, and taking sides in court rivalries.
Do not wear clothes or imitate lifestyle matching the king's royal status.
Wear only royal gifts with humble gratitude.
Smile only when appropriate, following the lord's mood.
Remain mute when secrets of others are discussed.
Show patience, composure, and sweetness regarding one's own vulnerabilities.
Do not elevate oneself excessively through extreme effort.
For a fall from height causes great sorrow, whereas high elevation alone gives no lasting joy.
Serving near a king is like playing with fire, weapons, or venomous snakes.
It can be sustained only through great skill, humbleness, and vigilance.
Having attained rare wealth and royal honor, stay vigilant to enjoy it long.
Arrange protective charms and sacred mustard around the king's bed.
Keep sacred ash, white mustard, and unhusked rice at night.
Place barley sprouts and protective charms near the headboard.
Keep a full water pot decorated with fresh flowers, fruits, and leaves.
Offer evening worship offerings after meals and at night.
Perform these auspicious rituals with purity and promptness.
This bestows long life, nourishment, and destroys evil spirits, poison, and sorcery.
This brief outline for poison protection is stated here; detailed procedures appear in the Supplementary Section (उत्तरस्थान/utttarasthana).
A wise physician should examine all signs of disorders and apply these remedies carefully.
The king who follows these teachings for protection against poison, artificial toxins, and evil forces with constant vigilance,
Remains unconquerable by enemy hordes and brings long-lasting peace and prosperity (क्षेम योग/peace and prosperity) to his people.

Thus ends the eighth chapter (अष्टमः अध्यायः/eighth chapter) named 'Food Safety and Protection' (अन्नरक्षाविधिः/food safety) of Ashtanga Sangraha.




Now, therefore, we shall explain the chapter (अध्याय/section) on the knowledge of incompatible foods (विरुद्धान्न/incompatible diet).
One should not consume together domestic meat (ग्राम्य मांस/domestic meat), marshy land meat (आनूप/marshy meat), aquatic meat (औदक/aquatic meat) with honey (मधु/honey), jaggery (गुड/jaggery), sesame (तिल/sesame), milk (पयः/milk), black gram (माष/black gram), radish (मूलक/radish), lotus stalk (बिस/lotus stalk), or germinated grains (विरुद्ध धान्य/incompatible grains).
Especially, fish (मत्स्य/fish) should never be consumed together with milk (पयः/milk).
Because both have sweet taste and post-digestive effect (मधुर रस विपाक/sweet taste and post-digestion), cause excessive fluid obstruction (अत्यभिष्यंदि/channel blocker), and have opposing cold and hot potencies (शीतोष्ण वीर्य/cold and hot potency), making them mutually incompatible (परस्पर विरुद्ध/mutually opposed).
Among all fishes, especially the Chilichima fish (चिलिचिम मत्स्य/chilichima fish) is strictly incompatible with milk.
That Chilichima fish has scales (शकली/scaly), red eyes (लोहित नयन/red eyed), red streaks all over its body (लोहित राजि/red striped), and usually moves near the ground (भूमौ विचरति/bottom dweller).
Due to its extreme channel-blocking nature (अत्यभिष्यंदितम/severe blocking), it strongly produces diseases (व्याधि/illnesses) and undigested toxic metabolic poison (आमविष/toxic digestive waste).
All sour substances (अम्ल/sour items), whether liquid or solid, are incompatible when mixed with milk (पयः/milk) or taken immediately after milk, and so are sour fruits (अम्ल फल/sour fruits).
Foxtail millet (कंगु/foxtail millet), Kodo millet (वरक/kodo millet), Makushta beans (मकुष्ट/moth bean), horse gram (कुलत्थ/horse gram), black gram (माष/black gram), and flat beans (निष्पाव/broad beans) are also incompatible with milk.
After consuming green leafy vegetables like radish (मूलक हरितक/radish greens), milk should not be drunk due to the fear of skin diseases (कुष्ठ/skin disorders).
Lotus leaf vegetable (पौष्कर शाक/lotus leaf vegetable), Rohini vegetable (रोहिणीक/rohini greens), or Jatuka vegetable (जातुंक/jatuka greens) should not be eaten along with honey and milk (मधु पयः/honey and milk).
Pigeon meat (कपोत मांस/pigeon meat) fried in mustard oil (सर्षप तैल/mustard oil) should not be consumed with honey and milk.
Similarly, fish meat (मत्स्य/fish) and wild boar meat (वराह/wild boar) fried in mustard oil should not be taken.
Jujube fruit (बदर/jujube), porcupine meat (श्वावित्/porcupine), and pork meat (वराह/pork) should not be eaten combined together.
Uncooked raw meat (आम मांस/raw meat) mixed with animal bile (पित्त/bile) is incompatible.
Chicken meat (कुक्कुट/chicken) or spotted deer meat (पृषत/spotted deer) should not be consumed with curd (दधि/yogurt).
Mutton meat (औरभ्र/sheep mutton) should not be eaten with safflower leafy vegetable (कुसुंभ शाक/safflower greens).
Sesame sweet cakes (तिल शष्कुली/sesame cakes) should not be taken with sour wine ferment (सौवीरक/sour barley wine).
Salt (लवण/salt) should not be mixed directly or taken with milk (क्षीर/milk).
Black gram soup (माष सूप/black gram lentil) should not be consumed along with radish (मूलक/radish).
Raw tender vegetable greens (मूल शाक/tender greens) should not be eaten with fresh butter (नैर्नीत/fresh butter).
Upodika spinach (उपोदका/spinach) should not be prepared or consumed with Maireya or Mardika wines (मैरेय मार्द्दीक/fermented wines).
Piluni fruit (पीलु/pilu fruit) should not be eaten with Karira sprouts (करीर/capers).
Sprouted grains (विरूढक/germinated sprouts) should not be eaten with lotus stalks (बिस/lotus stems).
Lakucha fruit (लकुच/lakucha fruit) should not be eaten with curd (दधि/yogurt), black gram soup (माष सूप/black gram), jaggery (गुड/jaggery), honey (मधु/honey), or ghee (घृत/ghee).
Banana fruit (कदली फल/banana) should not be eaten with curd (दधि/yogurt), buttermilk (तक्र/buttermilk), or Palmyra palm fruit (ताल फल/palm fruit).
Nightshade berries (काकमाची/solanum nigrum) should not be cooked or eaten with long pepper (पिप्पली/long pepper), black pepper (मरिच/black pepper), honey (मधु/honey), or jaggery (गुड/jaggery).
That same Kakamachi should not be eaten if cooked in a vessel previously used for cooking fish (मत्स्य पचन/fish cooking vessel) or ginger (शृंगबेर/ginger vessel).
Nor should Kakamachi cooked with those spices be eaten after keeping overnight (रात्रि मुषित/overnight stored).
Ghee (सर्पिः/clarified butter) stored in a bronze vessel (कांस्य भाजन/bronze vessel) for ten nights or more becomes toxic and incompatible.
Hot honey (उष्ण मधु/heated honey) or honey taken by a person afflicted with heat (उष्णार्त्त/heat suffering) or in hot weather is lethal like poison (विष/poison).
Ghee (घृत/ghee) and honey (मधु/honey) mixed in equal quantities by weight (समांश/equal proportions) become toxic and incompatible.
Hot water (उष्ण जल/warm water) consumed after taking honey (मधु/honey) or rain water taken after honey is incompatible.
Bhallataka marking nut (भल्लातक/marking nut) taken with hot water (उष्ण जल/hot water) causes severe burning and toxemia.
Kampillaka herb (कंपिल्लक/kampillaka) cooked with buttermilk (तक्र/buttermilk) is incompatible.
Baka crane bird meat (बक/crane meat) fried in pork fat (वराह वसा/pork fat) is incompatible.
Pigeon meat (कपोत/pigeon) cooked with mustard oil (सर्षप तैल/mustard oil) and iron wood timber smoke is toxic.
Krkavaku wild rooster (कृकवाकु/wild rooster) cooked with Eranda castor wood oil fire (एरंड/castor wood) is harmful.
Meat cooked with castor wood fire (एरंड दारु/castor wood) and castor oil is incompatible.
Peacock meat (मयूर/peacock) cooked on Haridra turmeric wood fire (हरिद्रा दारु/turmeric wood) is toxic.
Partridge meat (तित्तिर/partridge) cooked on Kampa wood fire or with mustard oil is incompatible.
Clarified butter (घृत/ghee) heated repeatedly or stored in iron vessels becomes unwholesome.
Equal quantities of rainwater (अंतरिक्ष जल/sky water) and sesame oil (तिल तैल/sesame oil) mixed together are incompatible.
Roasted barley flour (सक्तु/saktu flour) taken dry without water or taken late at night (रात्रौ/night time) is incompatible.
Taking saktu flour after eating a heavy meal (भुक्तवा/after meal) or drinking water between swallows is improper.
Eating saktu twice in a day or eating saktu without adding unctuous ghee/oil (अस्निग्ध/oil-free) is unwholesome.
Long pepper (पिप्पली/long pepper) fried in fish oil (मत्स्य तैल/fish oil) is toxic.
Mixing cold and hot items (शीतोष्ण/cold and hot), fresh and old grains (नव पुराण/new and old), or raw and cooked foods (आम पक्व/raw and cooked) together is incompatible.
Bathing in cold water (सलिलाभ्यवगाह/cold water bath) immediately when overheated by sun or exertion damages skin and eyesight (त्वग्दृष्टि उपघात/skin and vision injury) and increases thirst (तृष्णा/thirst).
Similarly, drinking milk immediately when exhausted by heat causes bleeding disorders (रक्तपित्त/bleeding disease).
Eating food suddenly when exhausted by physical overexertion (शरीर आयास/physical exertion) causes vomiting (छर्दि/vomiting) or abdominal tumor (गुल्म/abdominal tumor).
Eating food immediately after loud shouting or vocal strain causes voice loss (स्वरसाद/hoarseness of voice).
Thus, the principal examples of food item incompatibilities (अन्नपान विरोध/dietary incompatibilities) frequently used in daily life have been stated.
Regarding medicinal drugs (भेषज द्रव्य/medicinal substances), their usage according to proper prescription is most appropriate.
Detailed incompatibilities of all medicines are not stated here to avoid excessive length (अतिप्रसंग/over-expansion).
Nor is their knowledge absolute in every single condition (एकंतभद्रक/universally applicable).
Furthermore (अपिच/moreover):
Substances that aggravate bodily humors (उत्क्लिश्य दोष/humor aggravating) but do not expel them from the body (अहरेत्/non-eliminating) are termed incompatible (विरुद्ध/incompatible).
They act as direct enemies to bodily tissue elements (धातु प्रत्यनीक/tissue adversaries).
Incompatibility arises due to opposing qualities (गुण विरोध/quality opposition), unequal or equal combinations (विषम सम/unequal or equal ratio), or processing variations.
Incompatibility also occurs by processing (संस्कार/processing), place (देश/locality), season (काल/season), or inherent nature (निसर्ग/natural potency).
Milk with horse gram (क्षीर कुलत्थ/milk and horse gram), jackfruit with fish (पनस मत्स्य/jackfruit and fish), curd with honey (दधि मधु/yogurt and honey), and equal parts honey and ghee (क्षौद्र घृत समांश/equal honey and ghee) are toxic.
Drinking water after eating saktu at night (रात्रि सक्तु/night saktu), or consuming javaka grain with incompatible oils causes disease.
These examples are given merely as illustrations (उदाहरन मात्रक/representative examples) for intelligent physicians (कुशाग्र धी/keen intellects).
A wise physician expands these principles everywhere by applying clinical reasoning (क्रमते/applies logic).
Incompatible diet causes skin eruptions (विस्फोट/blisters), swelling (शोफ/edema), intoxication (मद/fainting), abscess (विद्रधि/abscess), abdominal tumor (गुल्म/tumor), and tuberculosis (यक्ष्मा/consumption), destroying digestive fire, energy, memory, intellect, and senses (तेजो बल स्मृति मन नाश/loss of vitality and mind).
Incompatible food produces fever (ज्वर/fever), bleeding disorders (अस्त्रपित्त/hemorrhage), eight major diseases (अष्ट महागद/eight major diseases), and even death like poison (विषवत् मृत्यु/poisonous death).
For those afflicted, prompt purificatory therapies (संशुद्धि/emesis and purgation) or pacifying treatments (शमन/palliative care) using opposing substances should be administered quickly.
Or the body should be previously prepared and immunized (शरीर अभिसंस्कृति/body conditioning) using those same substances gradually.
Incompatible food does not cause harm to those who perform regular physical exercise (व्यायाम/exercise), who are unctuous (स्निग्ध/well-nourished), have strong digestive fire (दीप्ताग्नि/strong appetite), are young (वयस्थ/youthful), and possess strong physical strength (बलशाली/physically robust).
Similarly, incompatible food causes no illness when consumed in very small quantities (अल्प भोजन/small quantity) or when habituated over time (सात्म्य/accustomed diet).
When administered opposing the disease state (दोष वैपरीत्य/opposite to humor), even improper substances can relieve patient suffering (रुज हरति/relieves pain).
Combining curd and milk (दधि दुग्ध/yogurt and milk) in specific therapeutic formulations is not considered harmful.
Even wholesome foods (पथ्य/wholesome foods) become unwholesome due to improper combination, dosage, or processing.
When those improper combinations are corrected, their incompatibility is removed (विरोध निवर्त्तते/incompatibility ceases).
Unwholesome habits (अहित सात्म्य/harmful habituation) should be abandoned gradually by one-quarter steps (पादांश/one-fourth portion), while wholesome habits should be adopted similarly.
Abandoning unwholesome habits in gaps of one day (एकांन्तर/one day interval), two days (द्वांतर/two day gap), or three days (त्र्यंतर/three day gap) ensures smooth transition.
When unwholesome habits are relinquished gradually in this sequence, bodily humors are not aggravated (दोष न संवर्द्धिता/humors unaggravated) and good health qualities flourish (गुण प्रभवंति/health qualities thrive).
Such gradually established health practices do not cause disease and remain firmly stable (स्थिरात्मतां प्राप्नुवंति/achieve lasting stability).
The three sub-pillars of life (त्रि उपस्तंभ/three supporting pillars) are food (आहार/diet), sleep (स्वप्न/sleep), and regulated sexual conduct (अब्रह्मचर्य/controlled celibacy).
When supported properly by these three pillars with proper logic (युक्ति युक्त/properly applied), the body gains physical strength, complexion, and nourishment (बल वर्ण उपचय/strength, skin tone, and growth), lasting full lifespan.
Among these, food has been stated and will be described further; now sleep (निद्रा/sleep) shall be explained.
Sleep originates from time and nature (काल सर्ग/natural time), rooted in darkness (तमो मूल/tamoguna origin), and is composed of tamo quality.
Due to the predominance of tamo quality at night (तमः बाहुल्य/excess darkness at night), sleep naturally occurs in the night.
When body channels are coated with kapha humor (श्लेष्मा वृत्त स्रोत/kapha filled channels) and senses are fatigued from exhaustion (इंद्रिय श्रम/sensory fatigue), sleep comes.
When sensory organs withdraw from their active functions (इंद्रिय उपरत/sensory rest), sleep enters the human body.
When all sensory organs are rested but the mind remains active among sense objects (मन अनुपरत/active mind), one sees various forms of dreams (स्वप्न/dreams).
One sees diverse dream visions during sleep due to mental impressions stored in memory.
Happiness (सुख/happiness), sorrow (दुःख/sorrow), nourishment (पुष्टि/emaciation), weakness (कार्श्य/weakness), physical strength (बल/strength), impotence (क्लीबता/virility), knowledge (ज्ञान/wisdom), ignorance (अज्ञान/ignorance), and life itself depend on sleep.
Proper sleep sustains life, strength, virility, and mental clarity.
Sleep indulged at improper time (अकाल निद्रा/untimely sleep) or in excess destroys happiness and longevity like another night of death (कालरात्रि/night of destruction).
Untimely and excessive sleep destroys bodily health and reduces lifespan.
That same sleep, when enjoyed properly in proper measure, endows the body with happiness and long life, like pure intellect in yogis (बुद्धि निर्मल/clear intellect).
Just as spiritual practice purifies the mind of ascetics, proper sleep revitalizes human body.
Remaining awake at night (रात्रौ जागरण/night awakening) causes dryness (रूक्ष/dryness), sleeping during daytime (दिवास्वप्न/day sleep) causes unctuousness (स्निग्ध/unctuousness), and napping while sitting upright (आसीन प्रचलायित/sitting nap) is neither dry nor unctuous.
Sitting naps cause no increase in kapha or dryness.
In summer season (ग्रीष्म ऋतु/summer season), due to vata accumulation, absorption of moisture, atmospheric dryness, and short nights, day sleep (दिवास्वप्न/daytime sleep) is wholesome.
In other seasons, sleeping during daytime aggravates kapha and pitta humors (कफ पित्त कर/kapha pitta aggravator).
Except those exhausted by singing (भाष्य/talking), riding vehicles (यान/traveling), long walking (अध्व/walking), wine drinking (मद्य/alcohol), women/sexual act (स्त्री/intercourse), carrying heavy loads (भार/weight lifting), or physical labor (कर्म/work);
And those overcome by anger (क्रोध/anger), grief (शोक/grief), fear (भय/fear), asthma (श्वास/breathing difficulty), hiccups (हिध्मा/hiccups), or diarrhea (अतिसार/diarrhea);
And elderly people (वृद्ध/aged), children (बाल/children), weak emaciated persons (अबल क्षीण/frail people), those afflicted by hunger, thirst, or abdominal pain (क्षुत् तृट् शूल/hunger thirst pain);
And those suffering from indigestion (अजीर्ण/indigestion), chest trauma (अभिहत/injury), insanity (उन्मत्त/madness), or those accustomed to day sleep (दिवास्वप्न उचित/habituated to day sleep);
For all these persons, day sleep maintains balance of tissue elements (धातु साम्य/tissue equilibrium) and nourishes body organs (अंग पुष्यति/body nourishment).
Persons with excess fat and kapha (बहु मेदः कफ/fatty kapha body) and those taking unctuous food daily should never sleep during daytime.
Persons afflicted by poison (विषार्त्त/poisoned) and throat diseases (कंठ रोगी/throat ailment) should never sleep, even during night time.
Day sleep in unsuited persons causes jaundice (हलीमक/jaundice), head heaviness (शिरो जाड्य/head stiffness), numbness (स्तैमित्य/stiffness), and heaviness of body (गुरु गात्रता/body heaviness).
It produces fever (ज्वर/fever), dizziness (भ्रम/vertigo), loss of intellect (मतिभ्रंश/memory loss), blockage of body channels (स्रोतोरोध/channel obstruction), and weak digestive fire (अग्निमंदता/low digestion).
It causes swelling (शोफ/edema), anorexia (अरोचक/loss of appetite), nausea (हृल्लास/nausea), chronic rhinitis (पीनस/rhinitis), and hemicrania headache (अर्द्धावभेदक/migraine).
It also gives rise to itching (कंडू/itching), skin rashes (कोठ/urticaria), eruptions (पिटका/pimples), cough (कास/cough), drowsiness (तंद्रा/drowsiness), and internal organ disorders.
It accelerates the urge and speed of toxic poison (विषवेग/poison impulse) in poisoned patients.
Due to undigested food waste, excessive kapha humor obstructs body channels (स्रोतांसि आवृणुते/obstructs channels).
When body channels are blocked internally, heaviness of body organs (गात्र गौरव/body heaviness) manifests.
Body heaviness leads to laziness (आलस्य/lethargy), and laziness leads to excessive sleeping (अतिनिद्रता/hypersomnia).
Purgation (विरेक/purgation), head evacuation (काय शिरो विरेचन/head cleansing), vomiting (वमन/emesis), and bloodletting (रक्तमोक्षण/bloodletting);
Medicinal smoking (धूम/smoking), hunger (क्षुत्/hunger), thirst (तृट्/thirst), grief (शोक/sorrow), joy (हर्ष/joy), sexual intercourse (मैथुन/sex), fear (भय/fear), and anger (क्रोध/anger);
Mental anxiety (चित्त उत्कंठा/mental worry), uncomfortable bed (असुख शय्या/uncomfortable bed), mental firmness (सत्त्व औदार्य/strong willpower), and conquering darkness/tamoguna (तमो जय/overcoming tamo quality);
And dry food diet (रूक्ष अन्न/dry food) cure excessive sleepiness and restore natural alertness.
These same factors should be understood as the primary causes of loss of sleep / insomnia (निद्रानाश हेतु/causes of insomnia).
Natural aging time (काल क्षयो/aging effect), depletion of tissues (शील क्षय/tissue loss), disease state (व्याधि/disease), and aggravation of vata and pitta humors (अनिल पित्त वृद्धि/vata pitta increase) cause sleeplessness.
Loss of sleep causes body aches (अंगमर्द/body pain), head heaviness (शिरोगौरव/head heaviness), yawning (जृंभिका/yawning), stiffness, and exhaustion.
It produces dullness (जाड्य/dullness), fatigue (ग्लानि/exhaustion), indigestion (अपक्ति/indigestion), drowsiness (तंद्रा/drowsiness), and severe vata diseases (वातज रोग/vata disorders).
When kapha humor is diminished and vata-pitta humors are increased, sleeplessness arises.
To cure insomnia, one should practice oil massage (अभ्यंग/oil massage), head oil pouring (मूर्द्ध तैल/head oiling), body massage (उद्वर्त्तन/body rubbing), bathing (स्नान/bath), and consuming wheat, rice, sugarcane, and milk foods (क्षीर अन्न/milk diet).
Consuming unctuous meat soups (स्निग्ध मांसरस/fatty meat soup), fatty meats of marshy animals (आनूप मांस/marshy meat), curd (दधि/yogurt), milk with ghee (क्षीर सर्पिः/milk ghee), and sweet wines (मद्य/wine) induces good sleep.
Pleasant soothing scents (मनोज्ञ गंध/pleasing aromas), pleasing music sounds (शब्द/comforting sounds), gentle eye soothing salves, gentle head massages, comfortable soft bedding (सुख शय्या/soft bed), clean room, and timely sleeping induce refreshing sleep.
Proper sleep brings physical happiness, mental nourishment, strength, virility, sound health, and full lifespan (आयुः/long life).
Next, regulated sexual conduct (अब्रह्मचर्य/controlled sexual activity) and celibacy guidelines (ब्रह्मचर्य/celibacy) shall be explained.
One should refrain from sexual intercourse with prohibited women, women during menstruation (रजस्वला/menstruating female), women without desire, or women suffering from genital diseases.
Intercourse should be avoided with women who are pregnant (गर्भिणी/pregnant), hungry, thirsty, dirty, aged, or belonging to higher spiritual status.
One should avoid sexual act in holy places, under sacred trees, near temples, in cremation grounds, at crossroads, or during eclipse and holy twilight hours.
Intercourse during day time (दिवा मैथुन/daytime sex), during midnight, during dawn, or during full moon / new moon days should be avoided.
Excessive sexual indulgence (अतिमैथुन/excessive sex) causes depletion of semen and vital energy (शुक्र ओजः क्षय/loss of semen and ojas).
It gives rise to joint pain (संधिशूल/joint pain), backache (कटिशूल/back pain), fatigue (श्रम/exhaustion), breathlessness (श्वास/gasping), and early emaciation (कार्श्य/wasting).
In winter season (हेमंत शिशिर/winter season), one may indulge in sexual intercourse according to desire (कामचार/as desired) after taking aphrodisiac tonics.
In autumn and spring seasons (शरत् वसंत/autumn and spring), sexual act should be performed once every three days (त्र्यहात्/three day gap).
In summer and rainy seasons (ग्रीष्म वर्षा/summer and monsoon), sexual act should be practiced once every fifteen days (पक्षात/fortnightly gap).
After sexual intercourse, one should immediately take a warm bath (उष्ण जल स्नान/warm bath), drink sweet milk with sugar and ghee (क्षीर सर्पिः/milk ghee drink), or consume meat soup (मांसरस/meat broth).
This practice preserves physical strength (बल/strength), memory (स्मृति/memory), intellect (मेधा/intelligence), complexion (वर्ण/skin tone), and long life (आयुः/lifespan).
Properly regulated sexual conduct preserves semen tissue (शुक्र धातु/reproductive fluid) and prevents premature old age (अजरा/anti-aging).
Controlled sexual life preserves vital energy (*ojas*), physical stamina, and mental courage.
Indulging in sexual intercourse with improper women or at improper time leads to genital diseases, loss of strength, and premature death.
One should always protect semen fluid (शुक्र रक्षण/semen preservation) as it is the supreme essence of all bodily tissue elements (*dhatus*).
Next, seasonal routine guidelines (*ritucharya*) and lifestyle adaptations shall be detailed.
The solar year is divided into six seasons (षट् ऋतु/six seasons): Hemanta (early winter), Shishira (late winter), Vasanta (spring), Greeshma (summer), Varsha (monsoon), and Sharad (autumn).
The three seasons from Shishira to Greeshma constitute northern solstice / Uttarayana (आदान काल/absorption period).
During Uttarayana, sun rays and sharp winds absorb atmospheric moisture and reduce human physical strength daily (बल हानि/loss of strength).
The three seasons from Varsha to Hemanta constitute southern solstice / Dakshinayana (विसर्ग काल/release period).
During Dakshinayana, cool moon rays and rains restore atmospheric moisture and increase human physical strength daily (बल वृद्धि/strength gain).
Physical strength is lowest in summer and monsoon (अल्प बल/low strength), moderate in spring and autumn (मध्यम बल/medium strength), and supreme in winter (उत्तम बल/maximum strength).
In winter season (हेमंत/early winter), digestive fire becomes extremely strong due to trapped internal body heat (दीप्ताग्नि/strong digestion).
If proper heavy food is not supplied, strong digestive fire consumes body tissues (धातु पाचन/tissue burning).
Therefore, in winter, one should consume sweet, sour, and salty foods (स्वादु अम्ल लवण/sweet sour salty diet), unctuous foods, meats, milk preparations, and new wine.
One should practice oil massage (अभ्यंग/oil massage), head oiling, sun bathing (आतप/sunbath), warm housing, heavy warm clothing, and thick blankets.
In late winter / Shishira season (शिशिर ऋतु/late winter), similar winter regimen should be followed, avoiding cold exposures.
In spring season (वसंत ऋतु/spring season), accumulated kapha humor liquifies under warm sun rays, dulling digestive fire (अग्निमंदता/low appetite).
Spring kapha gives rise to seasonal cough, colds, allergy, heaviness, and spring fever (वसंतज व्याधि/spring disorders).
Therefore, in spring, one should undergo therapeutic emesis (वमन/vomiting therapy), dry gargling, nasal drops (नस्य/nasal drops), and medicinal smoking.
One should eat light, dry, pungent, bitter, and astringent foods (कटु तिक्त कषाय/pungent bitter astringent diet), old barley, wheat, and green gram.
One should practice physical exercise (व्यायाम/exercise), dry powder body massage (उद्वर्त्तन/dry rubbing), and avoid heavy, sweet, unctuous foods, curds, and day sleep.
In summer season (ग्रीष्म ऋतु/summer season), intense sun rays dry up bodily unctuousness and aggravate vata humor while weakening digestive fire.
In summer, one should consume sweet, light, cold, unctuous, and liquid foods (स्वादु शीत द्रव/sweet cold liquid diet), rice, milk, ghee, saktu drinks, and coconut water.
One should avoid salty, sour, pungent, hot food items, alcohol consumption, heavy exercise, and intense sun exposure.
One should rest in cool forest groves, near cold water fountains, under moonlit terraces, and wear light silk clothes with cooling sandalwood paste (चंदन/sandalwood).
In monsoon season (वर्षा ऋतु/rainy season), digestive fire is severely weakened and all three humors—especially vata—become aggravated (त्रिदोष प्रकोप/tridosha aggravation).
Rainy atmospheric moisture, sour water, and damp cold soil impair digestion and cause gas, indigestion, and diarrhea.
In rainy season, one should eat boiled old grains, warm wheat, roasted rice, honeyed drinks, soups cooked with ginger and rock salt, and light warm food.
One should undergo medicated enema therapy (बस्ति/enema therapy) to pacify aggravated vata humor.
One should avoid river water, unboiled water, day sleep, damp soil, heavy physical labor, and cold rain exposure.
In autumn season (शरत् ऋतु/autumn season), body accustomed to rainy cold suddenly gets exposed to warm autumn sun, severely aggravating pitta humor (पित्त प्रकोप/pitta aggravation).
Autumn pitta gives rise to hyperacidity, skin rashes, fever, jaundice, and bleeding disorders (रक्तपित्त/bleeding disease).
In autumn, one should consume sweet, bitter, astringent foods (स्वादु तिक्त कषाय/sweet bitter astringent diet), ghee, rice, green gram, sugar, and milk.
One should undergo therapeutic purgation (विरेचन/purgation therapy) and bloodletting (रक्तमोक्षण/bloodletting) to clear pitta and blood toxins.
One should drink water purified by sun rays during daytime and moon rays during night, known as Hansodaka water (हंसोदक/pure autumn water).
Hansodaka water is extremely pure, non-blocking, clean, clear, and soothing like nectar for the body.
One should avoid heavy meals, curds, oil, fat, harsh sun exposure, eastern winds, and day sleep during autumn.
By practicing proper seasonal routines (*ritucharya*), bodily humors remain balanced and seasonal diseases never arise.
A person who adapts food habits according to seasonal shifts preserves strong immunity and sound health throughout the year.
Failure to adapt diet and lifestyle to changing seasons leads to rapid humor aggravation and chronic diseases.
Proper seasonal regimen cleanses bodily channels, kindles digestive fire, and strengthens mind and body.
One should gradually transition from one season's routine to the next during seasonal junction periods (*ritu sandhi*).
The seven days of the outgoing season and the seven days of the incoming season constitute seasonal junction period (ऋतु संधि/seasonal transition).
During seasonal junction, one should gradually relinquish previous seasonal habits and adopt new seasonal practices step by step.
Sudden abrupt changes in diet or lifestyle during seasonal shifts give rise to acute seasonal diseases.
Gradual adaptation during seasonal junction ensures smooth humor transition without health disturbance.
A wise person constantly observes dietary rules, seasonal routines, and personal constitution to maintain health.
Dietary rules, exercise, sleep, and seasonal adaptations are the cornerstone of Ayurvedic preventive health.
Self-controlled persons who follow these guidelines enjoy full lifespan free from physical and mental illnesses.
Daily health routine (*dinacharya*) and seasonal health routine (*ritucharya*) together form the foundation of disease prevention.
The intelligent person should select foods based on individual constitution, digestive fire capacity, and seasonal strength.
Eating wholesome food in measured quantity at proper time is the best medicine for sustaining human body.
Unwholesome food eaten out of greed, anger, or ignorance is the root cause of all physical ailments.
Physical health enables achievement of righteousness (*dharma*), wealth (*artha*), desire (*kama*), and spiritual liberation (*moksha*).
Therefore, one should diligently protect bodily health by maintaining righteous diet and disciplined lifestyle.
Mental purity, truthfulness, non-violence, and self-control enhance physical health and longevity.
Practicing noble mental conduct (*sadvritta*) protects against psychological and psychosomatic disorders.
Noble conduct brings peace of mind, social respect, divine grace, and spiritual elevation.
One should avoid evil deeds, malicious speech, harmful thoughts, and unrighteous activities.
Respecting spiritual teachers, elders, physicians, and virtuous persons enhances positive energy and longevity.
Helping the poor, sick, needy, and distressed brings spiritual merit and mental serenity.
Controlling senses, anger, greed, ego, and passion preserves internal peace and physical vitality.
Maintaining personal hygiene, cleanliness, clean surroundings, and pure thoughts keeps diseases away.
Cleanliness of body, mind, clothes, dwelling place, and food items prevents infectious diseases.
One should avoid standing in dusty places, harsh winds, direct hot sun, and unhygienic environments.
One should avoid night travel, entering lonely ruins, standing under old trees, and walking in dark places.
One should speak truthful, gentle, pleasing, beneficial, and measured words in daily life.
One should refrain from harsh speech, false statements, backbiting, and unnecessary arguments.
Maintaining harmonious relationships with family, friends, and society fosters mental well-being.
Mental stress, anxiety, sorrow, and mental conflicts weaken immune strength and cause early aging.
Spiritual reflection, meditation, self-study, and devotion promote inner stability and emotional resilience.
A calm balanced mind supports proper functioning of bodily humors, tissue elements, and digestive fire.
Thus, combined discipline of diet, seasonal routine, personal conduct, and mental serenity leads to perfect health.
One should practice proper seasonal and daily routines in all seasons, whether early winter or unseasonal climate shifts.
Because continuous habituation to seasonal regimen counteracts health disturbances caused by cold, heat, and rain.
Seasonal routine (*ritucharya*) is specifically designed as an antidote against seasonal defects of severe cold, heat, and rain.
Therefore, in seasonal regimen, early winter (*hemanta*) and late winter (*shishira*) share similar therapeutic routines.
For all living beings, maintenance of lifespan (*ayus*) constantly depends on intelligent application of health measures (*yukti*).
Human physical strength and longevity depend on two factors: past fate (*daiva*) and present human effort (*purushakara*).
Actions performed in past lives constitute past fate (*daiva*), while actions performed in present life constitute human effort (*purushakara*).
Both past fate and present effort are categorized into three grades: superior (*shreshta*), medium (*madhyama*), and inferior (*avara*).
The harmonious combination of superior past fate and superior present effort leads to a long, happy, and fixed lifespan (*niyata ayus*).
The opposing combination of inferior fate and inferior effort leads to short, unhappy, and variable lifespan.
Medium combinations produce medium lifespan; now listen further regarding the interplay of fate and effort.
Weak past fate (*durbala daiva*) can be overcome and destroyed by strong present human effort (*balavat purushakara*).
Similarly, weak present human effort can be overcome and negated by powerful past fate (*balavat daiva*).
Observing this mutual interaction, some sages consider human lifespan measure to be variable (*aniyata ayus*).
Certain karmic actions mature at a fixed predetermined time (*kala niyata karma*) with powerful results.
Other karmic actions mature when activated by suitable present causes and environmental conditions.
Thus, human death is stated to be of two distinct types: timely death (*kala mrityu*) and untimely death (*akala mrityu*).
Therefore, this systematic instruction on wholesome (*hita*) and unwholesome (*ahita*) practices has been declared.
Vedic scholars state that there are one hundred and one ways of human death (*ekottara mrityu shata*).
Among those 101 types of death, only one is timely death (*kala mrityu*), while the remaining one hundred are accidental/untimely deaths (*agantu mrityu*).
Just as performative rituals like Shyena sacrifice performed against enemies result in self-destruction;
Where improper actions performed by individuals lead to their own physical destruction;
Similarly, a person desiring long life achieves longevity through proper health practices and protective rituals.
Therefore, one should protect oneself from all hazards; such is the eternal wisdom of traditional scriptures.
Buddhist scholars also declare four types of human death based on physical causes.
Even for persons endowed with fixed lifespan (*niyata ayus*), death occurs if health hazards are neglected (*avisama parihara*).
Neglecting health rules produces constant disease in some persons and sudden premature death (*akala mrityu*) in others.
Sudden weapons attack, trauma, or accidents cause direct visible untimely death (*pratyaksha akala mrityu*).
Encountering wild maddened elephants, wild horses, or dangerous animals causes sudden death.
Lightning strikes, toxic poisoned air, reckless dangerous acts, and eating poisonous food cause instant death.
Divine sages endowed with vision advise avoiding all such reckless hazards and dangerous situations.
Neither spiritual remedies alone nor rejuvenation therapies (*rasayana*) can protect a person who acts recklessly.
Reckless individuals cannot attain their full natural lifespan despite having access to medicines.
Wise cautious people who avoid dangerous lions, wild elephants, and hazards remain free from fear.
Building false protective walls or adopting superficial security without avoiding underlying dangers is futile.
For a person desiring long life, avoiding forbidden acts like adultery and violence is essential.
Just as a deadly serpent summoned by mantras does not spare a person who touches it without precaution;
Or a person bitten by a venomous snake dies if proper antidote therapy is omitted;
Similarly, a person suffering from severe collapse or acute disease dies suddenly without proper treatment.
Death does not occur by mere neglect if lifespan remains, nor does life continue if fatal collapse is unmanaged.
In a battle where thousands of soldiers fight daily and kill each other, lifespan varies based on protection and actions.
Virtuous persons who practice self-protection survive, while reckless persons perish.
Neither divine gods nor Ashvini divine physicians can cure a person whose natural lifespan has expired.
However, proper medical intervention prevents untimely death (*akala mrityu*) when lifespan still remains.
Just as unbaked clay pots and baked clay pots survive long or shatter quickly depending on careful handling;
Objects stored in pictures or vessels last according to proper preservation or neglect.
When untimely death (*akala mrityu*) is fully established across all medical scriptures and direct observation;
How can a wise person entertain doubt regarding the reality and prevention of untimely death?
When a person dies despite the presence of a qualified physician, proper medicines, and attentive nursing, that death is true timely death (*kala mrityu*).
When death occurs due to absence of medical care, improper diet, or hazards, it is untimely death (*akala mrityu*).
Just as a well-maintained chariot gradually wears out and breaks down after full service over time;
Similarly, the body of a self-controlled person gradually completes its full lifespan (*ayus*), while reckless persons perish prematurely.
Just as an oil lamp filled with clean oil (शुचि तैल/clean oil) and good wick (दशा/wick), protected from insects and strong wind (कीट वात अपीडित/wind insect protected);
Shines brightly and burns completely until oil is exhausted naturally (स्नेह संक्षयात्/oil exhaustion);
Whereas an unprotected lamp with contaminated oil or strong wind extinguishes prematurely;
Similarly, a human being who practices wholesome diet and care (*hita upachara*) lives full natural lifespan.
Therefore, setting aside all other secondary pursuits, one should first protect bodily health (*sharira palana*).
Because if physical body is destroyed, all worldly achievements and goals cease to exist for living beings.
Just as a ruler protects his city (*nagari*) and a charioteer protects his chariot (*ratha*);
Similarly, an intelligent person should always remain vigilant in protecting his own physical body (*swasharira*).
Observing dietary principles, individual constitution, and natural properties of food, one should consume wholesome meals (*hita ahara*).
Because physical body is born from and sustained entirely by food (*ahara sambhava*).
One should never suppress natural bodily urges out of fear, shame, embarrassment, greed, joy, or sorrow.
Because suppressing natural urges is the direct abode and root cause of all physical diseases (*sarva apadam padam*).
For a person practicing wholesome diet and lifestyle, the jaws of untimely death (*kala damstra*) cannot snatch vitality or lifespan.
Proper lifestyle enhances physical strength (*bala*), enthusiasm (*utsaha*), sensory sharpness (*indriya*), and long life (*ayus*).
Even if a person who has abandoned unwholesome habits suffers a disease due to inescapable past fate;
He remains free from guilt, knowing he performed his self-protective duty as a wise virtuous person.
When a disease arises due to unavoidable past fate that cannot be prevented by human effort;
The wise person should not grieve over it, recognizing the inevitable nature of karmic fruit.
Persons who indulge in wholesome food and regimen, practice noble conduct, and respect moral virtues live a heavenly life on earth.
Their life resembles immortal nectar (*amrita*), bringing happiness in both present world and afterlife.
A foolish person who indulges in soft sensory pleasures, strays from righteous path, and becomes slave to desires lives in misery.
Such a fool lives an unrewarding life, multiplying his own suffering and downfall.
Therefore, wise persons should conquer internal bodily enemies—disease humors and unwholesome desires—and live a long healthy life.
Knowing that sinful improper acts produce painful consequences in present and future;
An intelligent person, remembering the wise words of Sage Atreya, should never commit unwholesome actions.
Thus ends the ninth chapter (नवमः अध्यायः/ninth chapter) named 'Incompatible Foods' (विरुद्धान्नविज्ञानीयः/knowledge of incompatible diet) of Ashtanga Sangraha.




Now, therefore, we shall explain the chapter (अध्याय/section) on the rules of taking food and liquids (अन्नपान विधि/dietary guidelines).
Food and drink (अन्नपान/dietary intake) consumed according to proper rules (विधि/guidelines) and agreeable to senses is called the abode of longevity (आयुः/lifespan).
Upon proper food depend vital energy (ओजः/vital essence), radiant heat (तेजः/metabolic aura), tissues (धातु/body tissues), senses, strength, contentment, nourishment, intellect, and health (आरोग्य/disease-free state).
Internal digestive fire (अन्तरग्नि/metabolic fire) stays burning using this food fuel (इंधन/fuel source).
Digestive fire (अग्नि/metabolic fire) is indeed the root cause of sustaining the physical body (देह धारण/body maintenance).
A self-controlled person (आत्मवान्/mindful individual) should analyze origin, combination, processing, quantity, region, season, and usage rules (उपयोग नियम/dietary regimen) to adopt wholesome diet (हितकर आहार/healthy food).
By natural quality (स्वभाव/natural property), rain water, red rice (रक्तशालि/red rice), sixty-day rice (षष्टिक/shashtika rice), green gram (मुद्ग/moong dal), deer meat, and quail are light (लघु/easy to digest).
Milk (क्षीर/milk), sugarcane juice (इक्षु/sugarcane), black gram (माष/urad dal), and aquatic animal meats are heavy (गुरु/heavy to digest).
These foodstuffs undergo alterations (परिवर्तन/change in properties) through specific processing and combinations (संयोग/mixing).
Combination (संयोग/mixing) means the blending together of two or more dietary substances.
This combination initiates unique actions (विशेष प्रभाव/special action) that individual components do not possess alone.
Processing (संस्कार/culinary processing) is induced through water contact, fire heating, washing, churning, geographic location, season, steeping, and cookware vessels (भाजन/utensils).
Quantity (मात्रा/dosage) refers to total bulk measure (राशि/volume measure) or individual item proportions.
Habitat region (देश/geographic region) refers to the native growing place of food and living habitat of the consumer.
The consumer (उपयोक्ता/eater) should be examined regarding healthy or diseased status and body constitution (प्रकृति/dosha constitution).
Time (काल/time factor) depends on seasonal changes (ऋतु/seasons), disease state, and signs of digested or undigested food (जीर्णाजीर्ण/digestion status).
If previous food remains undigested (अजीर्ण/indigestion), newly eaten food mixes with unrefined food fluid (*ama rasa*), rapidly aggravating all three humors (त्रिदोष/three humors).
When previous food is digested (जीर्ण/fully digested), humors return to normal locations, natural urges of urine and feces pass freely, belching is clear, chest and channels are open, body feels light, and genuine hunger appears; food eaten then enhances lifespan, strength, and complexion (वर्ण/skin aura).
This precise state alone is the correct time (उचित समय/right time) for taking meals.
Eating past the proper time causes Vata humor stagnation (वात प्रकोप/vata aggravation), sluggish digestion (मन्द अग्नि/weak digestive fire), and destroys appetite (अन्न रुचि/food taste).
Regarding meal etiquette (उपयोग नियम/dining rules), one should eat after bathing, cleanly dressed, offering food to deities, ancestors, sacred fire, teachers, guests, and dependents, in a clean pleasant place, with clean hands and face, seated facing east, consuming wholesome warm fresh food without despising it.
Stale food (पर्युषित/stale food) should not be eaten, except dried meat preparations and pickled condiments.
Food should not be left incomplete, except curds (दधि/yogurt), honey (मधु/honey), ghee (घृत/clarified butter), water, roasted flour (सक्तु/saktu), vinegar, and milk pudding (*payasa*).
Furthermore, specific guidelines should be followed while eating meals.
Eat unctuous, light, warm food (उष्ण आहार/warm food), neither too slow nor too fast, without talking or laughing, with full concentration (तन्मना/mindful eating), analyzing one's own digestive capacity.
Unctuous, light, warm foods kindle digestive fire (अग्नि दीपन/appetite boosting), cleanse digestive tract (कोष्ठ/digestive canal), do not disturb body tissues, digest quickly, and aid downward movement of Vata (वात अनुलोमन/downward vata movement).
Unctuous food (स्निग्ध आहार/oily food) strengthens sensory organs (इन्द्रिय/sensory organs), nourishes body, delays aging (जरा/aging process), increases strength, and improves skin complexion (वर्ण/skin complexion).
Naturally light food (लघु आहार/light food) undergoes digestion easily without distressing humors or causing discomfort.
Even if consumed slightly in excess, light food produces minimal harm (अल्प दोष/minor harm).
Warm food (उष्ण आहार/warm food) enhances food relish (रुचि/taste appeal) and reduces excessive Kapha phlegm (कफ/phlegm humor).
Eating excessively slow (विलंबित/too slow eating) gives no satisfaction, leads to overeating, causes food to turn cold, and results in irregular digestion (विषम पाक/irregular digestion).
Eating too fast (अतिद्रुत/too fast eating), talking, or laughing while eating leads to choking, improper entry of food, rapid distress, and loss of dietary benefits.
Thus, one must thoroughly analyze one's own body suitability (आत्म परीक्षा/self assessment).
A person should daily observe with vigilance what is wholesome suitability (*satmya*) and unwholesome unsuitability (*asatmya*) for oneself.
Suitability (*satmya/हास्य/wholesome habit*) means dietary items that agree harmoniously with one's body nature due to habitual use.
Unsuitability (*asatmya/असात्म्य/unwholesome habit*) is that which causes discomfort and bodily disturbance.
Physicians describe suitability of multiple kinds based on constitution (प्रकृति/body constitution), age (वयः/age), habitat (देश/region), season (ऋतु/season), humor (दोष/humor), and disease (व्याधि/illness).
By therapeutic adaptiveness (उपशय/therapeutic relief), even items of opposite properties become suitable through gradual adaptation.
Similarly, items matching body properties can become unsuitable if they cause therapeutic discomfort (*anupashaya*).
Suitability (*satmya*) is categorized into three grades: superior (*pravara*), inferior (*avara*), and medium (*madhyama*).
Suitability to all six tastes (षड्रस/six tastes) is superior, suitability to a single taste is inferior, and suitability to moderate tastes is medium.
Superior suitability to all six tastes is recommended for balanced body humors (समदोष/balanced humors).
Unwholesome habits (*ahita*) should be discarded gradually by one-quarter steps (पादांश/quarter step method) as explained earlier.
Dietary items that maintain equal body tissues (धातु साम्य/tissue equilibrium) and balance disturbed humors are defined as wholesome (हितकर/wholesome).
Dietary items opposite to this are unwholesome (अहितकर/unwholesome).
Due to individual bodily variations, one should regularly practice wholesome foods and avoid unwholesome items.
Especially, one must avoid *Samashana* (mixing wholesome and unwholesome foods), *Adhyashana* (eating before previous meal digests), and *Vishamashana* (eating at irregular hours).
Eating wholesome and unwholesome foods mixed together at one meal is called *Samashana* (समशन/mixed eating).
Eating fresh food while previous meal is still undigested is called *Adhyashana* (अध्यशन/overeating over undigested food).
Eating improper quantity (*amatrashana*) will be described separately.
Eating too early or too late relative to proper mealtime is called *Vishamashana* (विषमशन/irregular meal timing).
While eating meals, one should consume liquids (पेय/drinkable fluids) during or after meals according to suitability.
Drinking post-meal liquids (*anupana/अनुपान*) provides nourishment, lubrication, easy digestion, and bodily satisfaction.
Cold water (शीतल जल/cold water) is ideal post-meal drink after rice preparations, milk, honey, wine, and poison conditions.
Warm water (उष्ण जल/warm water) is ideal after ghee, oil, heavy foods, roasted grains (*saktu*), and Kapha-Vata disorders.
Rice wash water (*tandulodaka*) is ideal after consuming potherbs and legume dishes.
Buttermilk (तक्र/buttermilk) or sour gruel (*dhanyamla*) is suitable after heavy fatty foods.
Meat broth (मांसरस/meat soup) or whey (*mastu*) is suitable for weak emaciated persons after meals.
Milk (क्षीर/milk) serves as an excellent post-meal drink for persons emaciated by fasts, long travel, talking, sexual activity, or illness.
Alcoholic wines (मद्य/wine) serve as suitable post-meal drink for those accustomed to meat and wine with weak digestion.
Honey-water (मधूदक/honey water) is recommended as post-meal drink for obese individuals (स्थूल/obese person) to reduce fat.
Clear broth or whey (*mastu*) is suitable for those seeking body firmness or suffering from digestion sluggishness.
Grape wine or fruit vinegars serve as ideal post-meal drinks after heavy meat preparations.
Appropriate post-meal fluid (*anupana*) brings rapid distribution of food nutrients throughout all body tissues (धातु/tissues).
Anupana (अनुपान/post-meal drink) should possess properties opposite to food eaten, yet not antagonistic to body tissues.
Suitable post-meal drink satisfies, refreshes, invigorates, nourishes, breaks down food mass, and aids smooth digestion.
Post-meal drinks are contraindicated in dyspnea (श्वास/asthma), cough (कास/cough), excessive salivation, chest injury, and hoarseness of voice.
In upper body disorders (*urdhva-jatru*), post-meal liquids aggravate phlegm (*kapha*) in stomach and throat.
After taking drinks, one should avoid loud talking, singing, long walks, sleeping, exposure to sun or fire.
Post-meal fluids should be restricted in diabetes (मैल/diabetes), throat diseases, eye disorders, and skin ulcers (*vrana*).
Cold water post-meal drink is suited during summer (ग्रीष्म/summer season) and autumn seasons.
Warm water post-meal drink is suited during winter (हेमन्त/winter season) and after flour dishes.
Details of liquid substances should be consulted as described in Chapter 6 (*dravya vijnaniya*).
Milk (क्षीर/milk) as post-meal drink is best after eating Shali rice, Shashtika rice, fasts, travel, speech, and exercise.
