Even though the same herbs overlap across multiple groups (द्रव्यसंकर/herb overlap), there is no conflict in group nomenclature.
Because a single herb (एक द्रव्य/single drug) is capable of performing multiple therapeutic actions (बहुकार्य/diverse actions).
Excluding salt taste (लवण रस/salty taste), the remaining five tastes are called *kashayas* (कषाय/pharmaceutical decoctions) in pharmaceutical preparations.
Because those five tastes form the base origin (यौनि/fundamental source) for pharmaceutical extract preparations.
In salt, pharmaceutical forms like resinous extracts, decoctions, and pastes (*kashaya*) cannot be prepared.
Moreover, salt lacks independent decoction utility (नैरर्थक्य/meaningless in extract form), making salt decoction preparations unviable.
Verse summary on the fifty major decoction groups follows:
Jivanti, Kakoli, Ksheerakakoli, Meda, Mahameda, Mudgaparni, Mashaparni,
Rishabhaka, Jeevaka, and Yashtimadhu constitute the life-promoting group (जीवनीय/vitalizer group).
Vatya, Bala, Ksheeravidari, Kakoli, Ksheerakakoli, Ikshu, Ashwagandha,
Ksheerini, Rajakshavaka, and Ksharadvaji form the body-nourishing group (बृंहणीय/weight-promoting group).
Haimavati, Chirabilva, Musta, Kushtha, Vacha, Haridra, Daruharidra,
Chitraka, Katuki, and Ativisha constitute the tissue-scraping group (लेखनीय/tissue-reducing group).
Arka, Eranda, Chitra, Chitraka, Chirabilva, Shankhini, Sarala,
Hemakshiri, Katuki, and Langali form the purgative-purgation group (भेदनीय/purgative evacuants).
Honey, Yashtimadhu, Prishniparni, Katphala, Lodhra, Priyangu, Dhataki,
Ambashtaki, Samanga, and Mocharasa constitute the fracture-healing group (संधान/tissue-union group).
Hing, Maricha, Amlavetasa, Yavani, Bhallataka seed nut,
Along with Panchakola herbs, forms the digestive-fire kindling group (दीपनीय/appetite-booster group).
Aindri, Atirasa, Ksheeravidari, RishyaproKta, Shaliparni, Bala, Atibala,
Ashwagandha, Katuki, and Rishabhaka make up the strength-promoting group (बल्य/strength enhancer group).
Chandana, Punnaga, Ksheeravidari, White sugar, Shveta-sariva, Yashtimadhu, Padmaka, Usheera,
Manjishta, and Krishna-sariva constitute the complexion-enhancing group (वर्ण्य/complexion booster group).
Hansapadi, Brihati, Kantakari, Draksha, Sariva, Ikshumula,
Katphala, Yashtimadhu, Pippali, and Vidarikanda form the voice-improving group (कंठ्य/voice-clearing group).
Vrikshamla, Badara, Dadima, Kuvala, Amra, Amrataka, Lakucha, Karamarda,
Matulunga, and Amlavetasa constitute the heart-pleasing group (हृद्य/cardiac toner group).
Shunthi, Chavya, Chitraka, Vidanga, Murva, Guduchi, Vacha, Musta,
Pippali, and Patola form the thirst-relieving group (तृष्णा-निग्रहण/thirst-subduing group).
Kutaja fruit, Bilva, Chitraka, Shunthi, Ativisha, Vacha, Chavya,
Dhanvayasa, Haritaki, and Daruharidra constitute the hemorrhoid-curing group (अर्शोघ्न/piles-curing group).
Khadira, Amalaki, Bhallataka, Haridra, Saptaparna, Karaveera,
Aragvadha, Vidanga, and Jasmine leaves form the skin-disease curing group (कुष्ठघ्न/leprosy-curing group).
Jatamansi, Aragvadha, Chandana, Sarshapa, Musta, Nimba, Kutaja, Yashtimadhu,
Daruharidra, and Karanja constitute the itching-relieving group (कंडूघ्न/itching-destroying group).
Shigru, Maricha, Kembuka, Vidanga, Kandira, Achyranthes, Nirgundi,
Gokshura, Ativisha, and Musakaparni form the worm-destroying group (कृमिघ्न/anti-parasitic group).
Manjishta, Shleshmataka, Haridra, Rasna, Shirisha, Trivrit,
Ela, Chandana, Kataka, and Nirgundi form the poison-neutralizing group (विषघ्न/antidote group).
Shali rice, Kusha, Kasha, Shashtika rice, Veerana, Darbha, Ikshu, Balikshu,
Gundra, and Utkata roots constitute the lactation-promoting group (स्तन्यजनन/galactagogue group).
Patha, Shunthi, Devadaru, Musta, Guduchi, Sariva, Indrajava, Murva,
Katuki, and Kiratatikta form the breastmilk-purifying group (स्तन्यशोधन/milk-cleansing group).
Meda, Kakoli, Ksheerakakoli, Vandaka, Jeevaka, Rishabhaka, Kulinga,
Jatamansi, Mudgaparni, and Mashaparni constitute the semen-promoting group (शुक्रजनन/spermatogenic group).
Kushtha, Elavaluka, Katphala, Kanda-ikshu, Samudraphena, Usheera,
Vasuka, Ikshuraka, and Kadamba resin form the semen-purifying group (शुक्रशोधन/semen-cleansing group).
Draksha, Kakoli, Ksheerakakoli, Guduchi, Yashtimadhu, Jeevaka, Vidarikanda,
Meda, Jivanti, and Shaliparni form the unction-assisting group (स्नेहोपग/oleation-booster group).
Shigru, Punarnava, Shveta-sariva, Krishna-sariva, Kulattha, Masha, Badara,
Yava, Tila, Arka, and Eranda constitute the sudation-assisting group (स्वेदोपग/fomentation-booster group).
Laja, Amra, Badara, Dadima, Yava, Shashtika rice, Matulunga, Usheera,
Jambu leaves, Amra leaves, and pure mud form the emesis-checking group (वमन-निग्रहण/anti-emetic group).
Shunthi, Dhanvayasa, Hiberam, Parpataka, Chandana, Guduchi,
Bhunimba, Musta, Patola, and Dhanyaka constitute the feverish-thirst relieving group (तृष्णा-शमन/thirst-pacifier group).
Brihati, Kantakari, Vandaka, Pushkaramula, Haritaki, Pippali, Karkatashringi,
Shati, Duralabha, and Badara seeds form the hiccup-relieving group (हिक्का-निग्रहण/anti-hiccup group).
Shyama, Anantamula, Padmaka, Katvanga, Padmakeshara, Lodhra,
Dhataki flower, Samanga, and Mocharasa form the bowel-binding group (पुरीष-संग्रहणीय/astringent bowel-binder group).
Jambu, Shallaki, Yashtimadhu, Nilotpala, Kacchura, Krishna-sariva,
Pure earth, Ksheeravidari, and Shalmali form the stool-coloring group (पुरीष-विरजनीय/stool-pigment altering group).
Jambu, Amra, Udumbara, Vata, Kapitana, Plaksha, Pippala, Ashmantaka,
Bhallataka, and Somavalka form the urine-reducing group (मूत्र-संग्रहणीय/antidiuretic group).
Kamala, Nalin, Kumuda, Yashtimadhu, Saugandhika, Dhataki, Priyangu flower,
Utpala, Shatapatra, and Pundarika constitute the urine-color altering group (मूत्र-विरजनीय/urine-pigment altering group).
Vrikshadani, Gokshura, Darbha, Utkata, Vasuka, Pashanabheda, Kusha, Kasha,
Gundra, and Erandamula form the diuretic urine-evacuating group (मूत्र-विरेचनीय/diuretic evacuant group).
Draksha, Amalaki, Punarnava, Shveta-sariva, Krishna-sariva, Duralabha, Haritaki, Pippali,
Karkatashringi, Tamalaki, and Kantakari form the cough-curing group (कासहर/anti-tussive group).
Chanda, Amlavetasa, Shati, Tamalaki, Tulasi, Hingu, Jivanti,
Pushkaramula, Ela, and Agaru constitute the dyspnea-relieving group (श्वासहर/anti-asthmatic group).
Draksha, Peelu, Parushaka, Manjishta, Sariva, Guduchi, Patha,
And Triphala herbs form the fever-curing group (ज्वरहर/anti-pyretic group).
Dadima, Falgula, Parushaka, Priyala, Yava, Shashtika rice, Ikshu, Badara,
Draksha, and Kharjura constitute the fatigue-relieving group (श्रमहर/fatigue-dispelling group).
Padmaka, Laja, Usheera, Yashtimadhu, Utpala, Sariva, White sugar, Hiberam,
Kashmarya fruit, and Chandana form the burning-sensation relieving group (दाह-प्रशमन/anti-burning group).
Tagara, Shunthi, Agaru, Vacha, Dhanyaka, Bhutika, Pippali,
Pippalimula, Shyonaka, and Agnimantha form the coldness-dispelling group (शीत-प्रशमन/anti-cold group).
Tinduka, Priyala, Bijaka, Saptaparna, Khadira, Kadara, Badara,
Arimeda, Aswakarna, and Arjuna constitute the urticaria-relieving group (उदर्द-प्रशमन/anti-urticaria group).
Kakoli, Ela, Usheera, Kantakari, Shaliparni, Prishniparni,
Chandana, Yashtimadhu, and Eranda form the body-ache relieving group (अंगमर्द-प्रशमन/anti-bodyache group).
Yavani, Maricha, Ajaji, Gandira, Ajagandha,
Along with Panchakola herbs, forms the colic-relieving group (शूल-प्रशमन/anti-colic group).
Honey, Yashtimadhu, Laja, Gairika, Priyangu, Mocharasa, Earthen pot-sherds,
Lodhra with sugar, constitute the hemorrhage-checking group (शोणित-स्थापन/hemostatic group).
Silajatu, Elavaluka, Katphala, Mocharasa, Ashoka, Padmaka, Shirisha,
Punnaga, Kadamba, and Vetasa form the pain-alleviating group (वेदना-स्थापन/analgesic group).
Katphala, Hingu, Choraka, Guggulu, Ashokarohini, Vayastha,
Arimeda, Jatamansi, Golomi, and Vacha constitute the consciousness-restoring group (संज्ञा-स्थापन/resuscitative group).
Aindri, Durva, Abhaya, Vishvaksena, Haritaki, Arishta,
Brahmi, Vatyapushpi, and Shatavari constitute the pregnancy-stabilizing group (गर्भ-स्थापन/fetal-promoter group).
Guduchi, Haritaki, Amalaki, Jivanti, Gaja-pippali, Shaliparni,
Mandukaparni, Atirasa, and Punarnava form the youth-preserving group (वयः-स्थापन/anti-aging group).
Thus, these 45 major decoction groups (*Mahakashayas*) are illustrated to combat diverse diseases (नानाविध व्याधि/various ailments).
The wise physician should formulate and adapt them according to specific disease states, stage, and clinical requirements.
Thus ends the fifteenth chapter (पञ्चदशः अध्यायः/fifteenth chapter) named 'Compilation of Major Decoctions' (महाकषायसंग्रहः/fifty major decoctions) of Ashtanga Sangraha.




Now, therefore, we shall explain the chapter (अध्याय/section) on the collection of various herb groups (विविध गण/diverse plant groups) of Ashtanga Sangraha.
Vidari (विदारी/kudzu tuber), Panchangula (पंचांगुल/castor), Vrishchikali (वृश्चिकाली/stinging nettle), Vrishchiva (वृश्चीव/white punarnava), Devahvaya (देवदारु/deodar cedar), Shurpaparni (शूर्पपर्णी/mudgaparni).
Two Panchamula groups (पंचमूल/five roots), Laghu-panchamula and Jivana-panchamula, Kandukari (कंडूकरी/cowhage), Gopasuta (गोपसुता/sariva), Tripadi (त्रिपादी/three-footed herb).
This Vidaryadi group (विदार्यादि गण/vidari herb group) is pleasing to the heart (हृद्य/cardiac tonic), nourishing (बृंहण/body builder), and pacifies vata and pitta humors (वात पित्त शमन/vata pitta reducer).
This herb group cures wasting disease (शोष/emaciation), abdominal tumors (गुल्म/phantom tumor), bodyache (अंगमर्द/body pain), dyspnea (श्वास/asthma), and cough (कास/cough).
Sariva (सारिवा/indian sarsaparilla), Usheera (उशीर/khus vetiver), Kashmarya (काश्मर्य/gambhari fruit), Yashtimadhu (मधुक/licorice root), and two cold herbs (शिशिर द्वय/cooling duo).
Yashtimadhu (यष्टी/licorice) and Parushaka (परूषक/phalsa fruit) cure burning sensation (दाह/burning heat), bleeding pitta (पित्तास्त्र/bleeding disorder), and excessive thirst (तृष्णा/thirst).
Padmaka (पद्मक/wild cherry), Pundra (पुंडरीक/lotus stem), Vriddhi (वृद्धि/growth tuber), Tugaksheeri (तुगाक्षीरी/bamboo manna), Parpataka (पर्पट/fumitory), Shringi (शृंगी/gall nut), Guduchi (अमृता/giloy)—these ten herbs are named Jivaniya group (जीवनीय/vitality group).
They promote breast milk production (स्तन्यजनन/lactation booster), cure vata and pitta disorders (वात पित्त शमन/vata pitta cure), and provide gratification, body nourishment, and longevity (बृंहण जीवन/nourishment and vital growth).
Parushaka (परूषक/phalsa), Triphala Vara (वरा/triphala), Draksha (द्राक्षा/raisins), Katphala (कट्फल/box myrtle), and Kataka fruit (कतक फल/clearing nut).
Rajavriksha (राजवृक्ष/golden shower), Dadima (दाडिम/pomegranate), and leafy greens cure thirst (तृष्णा/thirst), urinary disorders (मूत्रामय/urinary diseases), and vata humor (वात/wind humor).
Anjana (अंजन/antimony), Phalini (फलिनी/priyangu), Jatamansi (मांसी/spikenard), Padma lotus (पद्म/lotus), Utpala (उत्पल/blue waterlily), and Rasanjana (रसांजन/berberis extract).
Ela (एला/cardamom), Yashtimadhu (मधुक/licorice), Nagakeshara (नागकेशर/ironwood flower) cure poisons (विष/toxins), internal burning heat (अन्तर्दाह/inner burning), and pitta humor (पित्त/bile humor).
Patola (पटोल/pointed gourd), Katuki (कटुरोहिणी/picrorhiza), Chandana (चंदन/sandalwood), Madhusrava (मधुस्त्रव/sweet resin), Guduchi (गुडूची/giloy), along with Patha (पाठा/velvet leaf).
Cures kapha-pitta humors (कफ पित्त/kapha pitta), skin diseases (कुष्ठ/skin disorders), fever (ज्वर/fever), poisons (विष/toxins), vomiting (वमि/emesis), anorexia (अरोचक/loss of appetite), and jaundice (कामला/jaundice).
Guduchi (गुडूची/giloy), Padmaka (पद्मक/cherrywood), Nimba Arishta (अरिष्ट/neem), Dhanyaka (धान्यक/coriander), and Red Sandalwood (रक्तचंदन/red sandalwood).
Cures pitta-kapha fevers (पित्त कफ ज्वर/pitta kapha fever), vomiting (छर्दि/vomiting), burning heat (दाह/burning sensation), thirst (तृष्णा/thirst), and kindles digestive fire (अग्निकृत्/appetite booster).
Aragvadha (आरग्वध/golden shower), Indrayava (इंद्रयव/conessi seeds), Patalika (पाटला/trumpet flower), Nimba (निंब/neem), Guduchi (अमृता/giloy), Madhurasa (मधुरसा/murcha), Vikankata (स्रुववृक्ष/flacourtia), Patha (पाठा/velvet leaf).
Bhunimba (भूनिंब/kalmegh), Sairyaka (सैर्यक/barleria), Patola (पटोल/pointed gourd), two Karanjas (करंज द्वय/two pongamia species), Saptaparna (समच्छद/dita bark), Chitraka (अग्नि/leadwort), Sushavi (सुषवी/black cumin), Bana (बाण/barleria shoot), Ghonta (घोंटा/jujube variety).
This Aragvadhadi group (आरग्वधादि गण/aragvadha group) overcomes vomiting (छर्दि/vomiting), skin diseases (कुष्ठ/skin disorders), poisons (विष/toxins), and fever (ज्वर/fever).
It destroys kapha humor (कफ/phlegm humor), itching (कंडू/itching), diabetes (प्रमेह/urinary disorders), and cleanses septic stubborn ulcers (दुष्ट व्रण/septic wounds).
Asana (असन/vijaysar), Tinisha (तिनिश/sandan wood), Bhurja (भूर्ज/birch bark), Arjun Shwetavaha (श्वेतवाह/arjuna tree), Prakirya (प्रकीर्या/karanja variety), Khadira (खदिर/catechu), Kadara (कदर/white catechu), Bhandi (भंडी/sirisha), Shimshapa (शिंशपा/rosewood), Meshashringi (मेषशृंगी/gymnema).
Three cooling herbs (त्रिहिम/three cooling woods), Palasha (पलाश/flame of forest), Jongaka (जोंगक/aloe-wood), Shaka (शाक/teak), Shala (शाल/sal tree), Dhava (धव/axlewood), Kramuka (क्रमुक/areca nut), Kalinga (कलिंग/conessi bark), Chhagalakarna (छागकर्ण/sal variety), Ashvakarna (अश्वकर्ण/sal tree).
This Asanadi group (असनादि गण/asana group) conquers leucoderma (श्वित्र/vitiligo), skin diseases (कुष्ठ/skin disorders), excess kapha (कफ/phlegm), and intestinal worms (कृमि/parasites).
It destroys anemia (पांडुरोग/anemia), diabetes (प्रमेह/urinary sugar), and fatty tissue disorders (मेदोदोष/obesity disorders).
Varana (वरण/crataeva bark), two Sairyakas (सैर्यक द्वय/two barleria species), Shatavari (शतावरी/asparagus root), Chitraka Dahana (दहन/leadwort), Morata (मोरट/root of sugarcane), Bilva (बिल्व/bael fruit), Vishanika (विषाणिका/horn-shaped pod).
Two Brihatis (बृहती द्वय/two nightshades), two Karanjas (करंज द्वय/two pongamia varieties), two Jayas (जया द्वय/two premna species), Bahalapallava (बहलपल्लव/thick leaves), Darbha grass (दर्भ/sacred grass), Rujakara (रुजाकर/pain-producing herb).
This Varanadi group (वरणादि गण/varana group) controls excess kapha (कफ/phlegm), body fat (मेदः/fat tissue), and sluggish digestion (मंदाग्नि/low digestive fire).
Cures stiffness of thighs (*adhyavata*), headache (शिरःशूल/head pain), abdominal tumors (गुल्म/phantom tumor), and internal deep abscesses (विद्रधि/internal abscess).
Tutthaka (तुत्थक/blue vitriol), Hingu (हिंगु/asafoetida), two types of Kaseesa (कासीस द्वय/green vitriol varieties), Saindhava rock salt (सैंधव/rock salt).
Along with Shilajit (शिलाजतु/mineral pitch), it cures dysuria (मूत्रकृच्छ्र/painful urination), urinary calculi (*ashmari*), abdominal tumor (*gulma*), excess fat (*medas*), and kapha.
Veeratara (वीरतर/dichrostachys bush), Aranika (अरणिक/premna root), Nala grass (नल/reed grass), Guntha (गुंठ/root variety), Morata (मोरट/sweet root), Tuntuka (टुंटुक/shyonaka), two Sairyakas (सैर्यक द्वय/barleria duo).
Mustaka (मुस्तक/nut grass), Manjari (मंजरी/flower cluster), Karkasha (कर्कश/rough leaf), Partha Arjuna (पार्थ/arjuna bark)—these ten herbs form the diuretic group (मूत्रविरेचन गण/diuretic class).
This Veeratiradi group (वीरतरादि गण/veeratara group) destroys all diseases caused by aggravated vata humor (वात व्याधि/vata disorders).
Cures urinary stones (अश्मरी/kidney stone), gravel in urine (शर्करा/urinary gravel), dysuria (मूत्रकृच्छ्र/painful urination), urinary retention (मूत्राघात/urinary blockage), and pain.
Lodhra (रोध्र/symplocos bark), Shabaraka-lodhra (शाबरक रोध्र/white lodhra), Palasha (पलाश/flame of forest), Jingini (जिंगिणी/gum tree), Sarala (सरल/pine wood), Katphala (कट्फल/box myrtle).
Kutsitamba (कुत्सितांब/bitter mango), Kadali (कदली/banana stem), Ashoka (अशोक/ashoka tree), Elavaluka (एलवालुक/red cherry seed), Paripelava (परिपेल्व/cyperus water grass), Mocha (मोच/silk cotton resin).
This Lodhradi group (रोध्रादि गण/lodhra group) destroys body fat (मेदः/fat tissue) and kapha humor (कफ/phlegm).
Cures uterine and vaginal disorders (योनिदोष/gynecological disorders), acts as astringent (स्तंभी/hemostatic binder), brightens complexion (वर्ण्य/skin illuminator), and destroys poisons (विष/toxins).
Arka (अर्क/calotropis), Alka (अल्क/white calotropis), Nagadanti (नागदंती/wild castor), Vishalya (विशल्या/gloriosa lily), Bharangi (भार्ङ्गी/pygmy flower), Rasna (रास्ना/pluchea bark), Vrishchikali (वृश्चिकाली/stinging herb), Prakirya (प्रकीर्या/karanja).
Pratyakpushpi (प्रत्यक्पुष्पी/apamarga), Peetataila (पीततैल/jyotishmati oil seed), Odakeerya (उदकीर्या/karanja), two Shwetas (श्वेता द्वय/two white flowered roots), Tapasa-vriksha (तापस वृक्ष/ingudi tree).
This Arkadi group (अर्कादि गण/arka group) removes excess kapha (कफ/phlegm), fat (मेदः/fat tissue), and poisons (विष/toxins).
It pacifies intestinal worms (कृमि/parasites), skin diseases (कुष्ठ/skin disorders), and specifically purifies septic ulcers (व्रणशोधन/wound cleanser).
Two Surasas (सुरस युग/tulsi varieties), Phanijjaka (फणिज्जक/wild basil), Kalamala (कालमाला/black basil), Vidanga (विडंग/false black pepper), Kharabuka (खरबुक/rough leaf), Vrishakarni (वृषकर्णी/mouse-ear herb), Katphala (कट्फल/box myrtle), Kasamarda (कासमर्द/negro coffee bush).
Kshavaka (क्षवक/sneezewort), Sarasi (सरसि/water lily), Bharangi (भार्ङ्गी/pygmy flower), Karmuka (कार्मुक/bamboo shoot), Kakamachi (काकमाची/black nightshade), Kulahala (कुलहल/celsia herb), Vishamushti (विषमुष्टी/nux vomica), Bhustrina (भूस्तृण/lemon grass), Bhutakeshi (भूतकेशी/spikenard variety).
This Surasadi group (सुरसादि गण/surasa basil group) destroys phlegm (श्लेष्मा/kapha), body fat (मेदः/fat tissue), and worms (कृमि/parasites).
Cures rhinitis (प्रतिश्याय/common cold), anorexia (अरोचक/loss of appetite), dyspnea (श्वास/asthma), cough (कास/cough), and purifies wounds (व्रणशोधन/wound cleanser).
Mushkaka (मुष्कक/tree ash), Dhruba (ध्रुव/stable wood), Varahi (वाराही/yam tuber), Pippali (पिप्पली/long pepper), Palasha (पलाश/flame of forest), Dhava (धव/axlewood), Shimshapa (शिंशपा/rosewood).
Cures abdominal tumors (गुल्म/phantom tumor), diabetes (प्रमेह/urinary sugar), kidney stones (अश्मरी/calculi), anemia (पांडु/anemia), obesity (मेदः/fat), piles (अर्शः/hemorrhoids), kapha, and semen defects (शुक्रदोष/semen disorders).
Vatsaka (वत्सक/kutaja bark), Madhurasa (मधुरसा/murcha), Truti Ela (त्रुटि/cardamom), Vacha (वचा/sweet flag), Dirghavrinta Shyonaka (दीर्घवृंत/shyonaka), Velva (वेल्ल/vidanga), Sarshapa (सर्षप/mustard).
Katurohini (रोहिणी/picrorhiza), Sthapani (स्थपनि/herbal anchor), Hingu (हिंगु/asafoetida), Bharangi (भार्ङ्गी/pygmy flower), Shailaghati Pashanabheda (शैलघाति/stone breaker), Ghanapriya (घनप्रिय/nut grass lover).
This Vatsakadi group (वत्साकादि गण/kutaja group) conquers vata and kapha humors (अनिल श्लेष्मा/vata kapha), excess fat (मेदः/fat tissue), anorexia (अरोचक/loss of appetite), and rhinitis (पीनस/catarrh).
Cures colic pain (शूल/abdominal colic), piles (अर्शः/hemorrhoids), fever (ज्वर/fever), abdominal tumor (*gulma*), and acts as appetite booster and digestive (दीपन पाचन/digestive fire stimulant).
Vacha (वचा/sweet flag), Jalada Musta (जलद/nut grass), Devadaru (देवाह्व/deodar cedar), Nagara Ginger (नागर/dry ginger), Ativisha (अतिविषा/aconite root), Abhaya Haritaki (अभया/myrobalan).
Two Turmerics (हरिद्रा द्वय/turmeric and tree turmeric), Yashtimadhu (यष्ट्याह्व/licorice), Kalashi (कलशी/prishniparni), Kutaja (कुटज/conessi bark).
Vchadi and Haridradi groups (वचा हरिद्रादि गण/vacha and haridra groups) digest undigested food toxins (*ama*) and cure diarrhea (*atisara*).
They destroy body fat (मेदः/fat tissue), excess kapha (कफ/phlegm), severe vata (*adhyavata*), and breast milk impurities (स्तन्यदोष/milk toxicity).
Priyangu flowers (प्रियंगु/fragrant berry), two Anjanas (अंजन युग्म/antimony and berberis extract), Padma (पद्म/lotus), lotus pollen (पद्म रज/lotus pollen), Yojanavalli Manjistha (योजनवल्ली/madder root), Ananta (अनंता/sarsaparilla).
Manadruma (मानद्रुम/large tree), Mocharasa (मोचरस/silk cotton gum), Samanga (समंगा/touch-me-not), Punnaga (पुन्नाग/calophyllum), Sheeta (शीत/cooling herb), Madaniya-hetu (मदनीय/intoxicating wood).
Ambashta (अंबष्ठा/velvet leaf), Yashtimadhu (मधुक/licorice), Namaskari (नमस्करी/sensitive plant), Nandivriksha (नंदीवृक्ष/ficus tree), Palasha (पलाश/flame of forest), Kachchura (कच्छुरा/cowhage).
Lodhra (रोध्र/symplocos bark), Dhataki flowers (धातकि/fire-flame bush), Bilva pulp (बिल्व पेशिका/unripe bael fruit), Katvanga (कट्फंग/shyonaka), lotus pollen (कमल रज/lotus pollen).
Priyangvadi and Ambashtadi groups (प्रियंगु अंबष्ठादि गण/priyangu ambashta groups) cure chronic dysentery and diarrhea (पक्वातीसार/matured diarrhea).
They act as bone and tissue healers (संधानीय/tissue binder), benefit pitta disorders (पित्त शमन/pitta pacifier), and heal open wounds (व्रणरोपण/wound healer).
Musta (मुस्ता/nut grass), Vacha (वचा/sweet flag), Chitraka Agni (अग्नि/leadwort), two Turmerics (द्विनिशा/turmeric duo), Katuki (तिक्ता/bitter root), Bhallataka (भल्लात/marking nut), Patha (पाठा/velvet leaf), Triphala (त्रिफला/three myrobalans), Ativisha (विषा/aconite root).
Kushtha (कुष्ठ/saussurea root), Truti Ela (त्रुटि/cardamom), Haimavati (हैमवती/white vacha)—these cure uterine diseases (योनि रोग/vaginal disorders), purify breast milk (स्तन्य विशोधन/milk purifier), and digest fecal waste (मलपाचन/waste eliminator).
Nyagrodha (न्यग्रोध/banyan bark), Pippala (पिप्पल/sacred fig), Sadaphala (सदाफल/gular fig), two Lodhras (रौध्र युग्म/two symplocos barks), two Jambus (जंबू द्वय/two jamun trees), Arjuna (अर्जुन/arjuna bark), Kapeetana (कपीतन/hog plum), Somavalka (सोमवल्क/white khadir).
Plaksha (प्लक्ष/fig tree), Amra (आम्र/mango bark), Vanjula (वंजुल/cane stem), Piyala (पियाल/chironji), Palasha (पलाश/dhak tree), Nandi (नंदी/ficus variety), Kadamba (कदंब/burflower tree), Virala (विरला/tendu tree), Madhuka (मधुक/mahua flower), Yashtimadhu (मधुक/licorice).
This Nyagrodhadi group (न्यग्रोधादि गण/banyan group) cleanses wounds (व्रण्य/wound purifier), acts as astringent (संग्राही/bowel binder), and unites fractured bones (भग्नसाधन/bone-fracture healer).
It destroys excess fat (मेदः/obesity), bleeding pitta (पित्तास्र/bleeding disorders), thirst (तृष्णा/thirst), burning sensation (दाह/burning heat), and gynecological diseases (योनि रोग/uterine disorders).
Two Elas (एला युग्म/two cardamoms), Turushka (तुरुष्क/incense resin), Kushtha (कुष्ठ/saussurea), Phalini (फलिनी/priyangu), Jatamansi (मांसी/spikenard), Hribera Jala (जल/khus vetiver), Dhyamaka (ध्यानक/scented grass), Sprikka (स्पृक्का/clover herb), Choraka (चोरक/angelica root), Chocha Patra (चोचपत्र/cinnamon leaf), Tagara (तगर/valerian), Sthauaneya (स्थौणेय/yew leaf), Jati (जाती/jasmine flower), Rasa (रस/myrrh resin).
Shukti (शुक्ति/pearl oyster shell), Vyaghranakha (व्याघ्रनख/tiger claw shell), Devadaru (अमराह्व/deodar cedar), Aguru (अगरु/aloe-wood), Shrivasaka (श्रीवासक/turpentine resin), Kumkuma (कुंकुम/saffron), Chandana (चंदन/sandalwood), Guggulu (गुग्गुलु/bdellium resin), Devadhupa (देवधूप/sal resin), Ravipura (रवपुरा/incense powder), Punnaga (पुन्नाग/calophyllum), Nagakeshara (नागाह्वय/ironwood flower).
This Eladi group (एलादि गण/cardamom group) controls vata and kapha humors (वात कफ/vata kapha) and overcomes poisons (विष/toxins).
It clarifies skin complexion (वर्णप्रसादन/skin illuminator) and destroys itching (कंडू/itching), boils (पिटिका/skin pustules), and allergic skin rashes (कोठ/urticaria).
Shyama (श्यामा/black trivrit), Danti (दंती/wild castor root), Dravanti (द्रवंती/croton root), Kramuka (क्रमुक/betel nut), Kutharana (कुठरणा/purgative bark), Shankhini (शंखिनी/conch herb), Charmasahva (चर्मसाह्व/purgative pod), Swarnakshiri (स्वर्णक्षीरी/yellow thistle), Gavakshi (गवाक्षी/bitter cucumber), Shikhari Apamarga (शिखरि/apamarga), Rajana (रजनी/turmeric), Chhinnaroha Guduchi (छिन्नरोहा/giloy), Karanja (करंज/pongamia).
Bastantri (बस्तांत्री/goat climber), Vyadhighata Aragvadha (व्याधिघात/golden shower), Bahala (बहल/thick bark), Bahurasa (बहुरस/juicy sugarcane), fruits of Snuhi (तीक्ष्णवृक्ष फल/purging milk hedge)—this Shyamadi group (श्यामादि गण/black trivrit group) destroys skin diseases (कुष्ठ/skin disorders), poison (विष/toxins), anorexia (अरोचक/loss of appetite), kapha, heart disease (हृद्रुज्/cardiac pain), and dysuria (मूत्रकृच्छ्र/painful urination).
Pippali (पिप्पली/long pepper), Pippalimula (पिप्पलीमूल/pepper root), Chavya (चव्य/java long pepper), Chitraka (चित्रक/leadwort), Bhringavera Ginger (भृंगवेर/fresh ginger), Maricha (मरिच/black pepper), Hastipippali (हस्तिपिप्पली/elephant pepper), Harenuka (हरेणुक/pea seed), Ela (एला/cardamom), Ayamoda (अजमोद/celery seed), Indrayava (इंद्रयव/conessi seeds), Patha (पाठा/velvet leaf), Jeeraka (जीरक/cumin), Sarshapa (सर्षप/mustard), Mahanimba (महानिंब/bead tree), Phalgu (फल्गु/wild fig), Hingu (हिंगु/asafoetida), Bharangi (भार्ङ्गी/pygmy flower), Vacha (वचा/sweet flag), Musta (मुस्ता/nut grass), Madhurasa (मधुरसा/murcha), Ativisha (अतिविषा/aconite root), Vidanga (विडंग/false black pepper), Katurohini (कटुरोहिणी/picrorhiza).
This Pippalyadi group (पिप्पल्यादि गण/long pepper group) destroys kapha humor (कफ/phlegm), rhinitis (प्रतिश्याय/common cold), vata humor (अनिल/wind), and anorexia (अरोचक/loss of appetite).
It acts as appetite booster (दीपन/digestion booster), cures abdominal tumors (गुल्म/phantom tumor), abdominal colic (शूल/colic pain), and digests undigested chyme toxins (*ama-pachana*).
Thus twenty-five herb groups (पंचविंशति गण/twenty-five plant classes) are described; in case of non-availability of any drug, substitute similar drugs and omit non-fitting ones.
One should substitute similar herbal drugs (तद्विध द्रव्य/similar substitute) and leave out incompatible herbs carefully.
These herb groups, considering humor (*dosha*) and tissue (*dushya*), prepared as paste (*kalka*), decoction (*kvatha*), medicated oils/ghee (*sneha*), or confections (*leha*),
Used internally for drinking, nasal drops (*nasya*), enema (*anvasana*), or externally as ointments (*lepa*) and oil massage (*abhyanga*), cure even most stubborn diseases (*sukrichhra roga*).
Thus ends the sixteenth chapter (षोडशः अध्यायः/sixteenth chapter) named 'Diversity of Herb Classes' (विविधगणसंग्रहः/diverse plant categories) of Ashtanga Sangraha.




Now, therefore, we shall explain the chapter (अध्याय/section) on the principles of substances, qualities, and tastes (द्रव्यादिविज्ञानीयः/knowledge of dravya and guna) of Ashtanga Sangraha.
In this universe, every physical substance (द्रव्य/material substance) is composed of five basic elements (पंचमहाभूत/five subtle elements).
Its substratum is Earth element (*prithvi*), its origin is Water element (*jala*), and Space, Air, and Fire elements cause distinct structural modifications.
Substances are named according to the predominance (उत्कर्ष/predominance) of particular mahabhuta elements contained within them.
Because of the combination of multiple elements (भूतसमवाय/elemental combination), no substance possesses only a single taste (*ekarasa*).
Similarly, diseases are also never caused by a single humor (*doshaja*) alone.
The primary taste (व्यक्त रस/manifested taste) is directly perceivable upon tongue contact.
The secondary taste (*anurasa*) is subtle or unmanifested initially, becoming perceivable only at the end of digestion or when uninhibited.
Tastes possess two main functional effects (कर्म/action): purificatory/scraping (*chhedana*) and pacifying/soothing (*upashamana*), alongside beneficial or harmful influence.
Substances based on Earth and other elements manifest qualities like heavy (*guru*) or light (*laghu*) depending on origin, processing, region, and season.
Earth-predominant substances (पार्थिव द्रव्य/earthy substance) are heavy, hard, clear, slow, dense, gross, stable, and fragrant, producing body stability, weight, and compactness.
Water-predominant substances (जलीय/aquatic substance) are liquid, unctuous, cold, heavy, slow, smooth, viscous, promoting moisture, softness, and contentment.
Fire-predominant substances (आग्नेय/fiery substance) are sharp, hot, dry, subtle, light, clear, and radiant, causing burning heat, digestion, illumination, and color brilliance.
Air-predominant substances (वायव्य/aerial substance) are dry, subtle, light, clear, non-viscous, cold, and rough, causing dryness, lightness, fatigue, and bodily movement.
Space-predominant substances (आकाशीय/ethereal substance) are soft, subtle, light, clear, non-viscous, smooth, causing softness, porosity, and lightness in tissues.
Thus, there exists no substance in this entire universe (जगत्/world) that cannot serve as medicine (औषध/therapeutic agent) when used appropriately.
Substances rich in Fire and Air elements generally possess an upward-moving inclination (ऊर्ध्वभाग/upward direction - emetic).
Fire naturally ascends due to lightness, and Air moves upwards due to its floating nature (*plavanatva*).
Substances rich in Earth and Water elements generally possess a downward-moving inclination (अधोभाग/downward direction - purgative) due to heaviness.
Substances containing mixed elemental compositions act in both upward and downward directions (*ubhaya-bhagahara*).
Pacifying substances (*shamana*) act by possessing qualities directly opposite to the aggravated humors (*doshas*).
In composite substances, nomenclature and properties are assigned based on the predominant element (*bahulya*).
Substances predominant in Air element possess bowel-binding/absorbent qualities (*grahi*).
Substances predominant in Fire element kindle appetite (दीपन/appetite booster) and digest chyme (*pachana*).
Substances possessing both Fire and Air elements perform scraping/attenuating action (लेखन/tissue scraping).
Substances rich in Earth and Water elements perform body-nourishing and bulk-promoting action (बृंहण/nourishing effect).
Pungent (*katu*), sour (*amla*), and salty (*lavana*) tastes are progressively hotter in thermal potency (*ushna veerya*).
Bitter (*tikta*), astringent (*kashaya*), and sweet (*madhura*) tastes are progressively colder in thermal potency (*sheeta veerya*).
Bitter, pungent, and astringent tastes are dry (रूक्ष/dry), binding feces, urine, and flatus.
Salty, sour, and sweet tastes are unctuous (स्निग्ध/unctuous), facilitating smooth passage of feces, urine, and flatus.
Salty, astringent, and sweet tastes are heavy for digestion (गुरु/heavy digestion).
Similarly, sour, pungent, and bitter tastes are light for digestion (लघु/easy to digest).
Some authorities consider salty taste as possessing moderate qualities regarding heavy, light, unctuous, and dry attributes.
Potency (*veerya*) is classified into eight types by some seers: heavy, light, unctuous, dry, sharp, slow, cold, and hot.
Other Ayurvedic masters state the fundamental definition of potency in classical verses:
'Potency (*veerya*) is that inherent power of a substance by virtue of which pharmacological action is executed.'
'No therapeutic action takes place without potency; all pharmacological actions are entirely driven by potency.'
Proponents of eightfold potency emphasize that among all attributes, these eight qualities possess supreme pharmacological strength.
They encompass and represent the ultimate potency dominating over tastes, post-digestive effects, and general attributes.
Hence, in clinical practice and pharmacological literature, substances are primarily described by these dominant potencies.
When a drug exhibits unique actions overriding all general taste and quality rules, that specific power is named *Prabhava* (प्रभाव/specific drug action).
Even when therapeutic actions appear similar, distinct qualities are designated under specific potencies (*veerya*) or unique drug actions (*prabhava*).
Other sages divide potency into two thermal types—hot (*ushna*) and cold (*sheeta*)—reflecting Agni (fire) and Soma (water) cosmic divisions.
They state that despite diverse attributes, all substances are dominated by Fire (*agni*) and Water (*soma*) cosmic forces:
'Though substances have diverse compositions, Fire and Water forces govern the entire universe, manifest or unmanifest.'
Nothing in this world ever transcends these two fundamental thermal poles of Fire (*Agni*) and Water (*Soma*).
Hot potency (*ushna veerya*) produces burning, digestion, sweating, liquefaction, exudation, and pacifies kapha humor.
Cold potency (*sheeta veerya*) provides refreshing joy, stanching/stiffening (*stambhana*), vitalizing, and pleases bleeding pitta.
Post-digestive effect (*vipaka*) generally turns sweet for sweet/salty tastes, sour for sour taste, and pungent for bitter/pungent/astringent tastes.
Post-digestive effect produces metabolic results similar to its corresponding tastes upon tissue assimilation.
One should evaluate the degree of post-digestive effect—mild, moderate, or intense—based on specific drug qualities.
Sage Parashara states his specific view on threefold post-digestive transformation:
'Transformation is threefold: sour taste becomes sour vipaka, pungent taste becomes pungent vipaka, and sweet taste becomes sweet vipaka.'
'Four other tastes transform accordingly, while mixed tastes yield combined post-digestive effects upon assimilation.'
If bitter, pungent, and astringent tastes all yield pungent vipaka, how could bitter and astringent tastes pacify Pitta humor?
Substances sweet in taste and vipaka with cold potency, sour substances with hot potency, and pungent substances with pungent vipaka follow standard rules.
Their effects on dosha aggravation or pacification can be inferred directly from their standard taste-potency-vipaka combination.
However, in substances with mixed attributes like milk, wine, or black pepper, actions alter due to specific combination factors.
For instance, honey (*madhu*) is sweet yet pacifies Kapha because of pungent vipaka, astringent sub-taste, and dry nature; it increases Vata due to cold potency.
Similarly, barley (*yava*) is sweet yet dry and pacifies Kapha while increasing Vata.
Marshy animal meat, though cold in touch, increases Pitta due to hot potency; sesame oil is pungent in vipaka, binding urine and feces.
Sour fermented gruel (*kanjika*) pacifies Kapha because of its dry and hot qualities.
Kapittha fruit pacifies Pitta because of its cold potency and dry nature.
Amalaki fruit pacifies Pitta humor because of cold potency and sweet post-digestive effect (*madhura vipaka*).
Sour pomegranate (*dadima*) pacifies Pitta because of mild unctuousness and non-heating nature.
Fish meat, though sweet in taste, aggravates Pitta humor because of hot thermal potency.
Ginger (*shunthi*) pacifies Kapha and Vata because of hot potency, unctuousness, and heavy qualities.
Onion (*palandu*) acts similarly, nourishing tissue strength.
Onion increases Kapha through unctuousness and heaviness, while tender radish is sweet in post-digestive effect.
Bitter herbs like Ativisha, Arka, and Aguru aggravate Pitta humor because of their hot thermal potency.
The astringent-bitter Brihat-panchamula pacifies Vata humor but does not pacify Pitta because of hot thermal potency.
Astringent horse gram (*kulattha*) aggravates Pitta because of its sour post-digestive effect (*amla vipaka*).
These examples are stated merely as illustrations to guide clinical understanding.
Therefore, Ayurvedic doctrine summarizes drug actions:
'A substance acts by virtue of its taste (*rasa*), another by its potency (*veerya*), another by its qualities (*guna*), another by its post-digestive effect (*vipaka*), and another by its specific action (*prabhava*).'
'If one among taste, potency, quality, or vipaka is exceptionally strong, it overrides weaker attributes and executes its action.'
'When opposing qualities meet within a substance, the stronger quality inevitably overcomes the weaker quality.'
'Post-digestive effect (*vipaka*) overrides taste (*rasa*); thermal potency (*veerya*) overrides both taste and vipaka; and specific action (*prabhava*) overrides all of them.'
This hierarchy of dominance represents the natural inherent strength (*naisargika bala*) of pharmacological attributes.
Even when attributes are mutually opposing, they co-exist within a substance to achieve therapeutic results without destroying one another.
Taste, potency, and vipaka reside in a drug depending on the predominance or weakness of elemental constituents (*bhuta*).
Attributes like sweetness, coldness, viscosity, unctuousness, heaviness, and slowness co-exist harmoniously in cow milk.
The same qualities mentioned for substances also apply to bodily humors (*doshas*) during their increase, decrease, or normal equilibrium.
Taste (*rasa*) is perceived upon tongue contact; potency (*veerya*) is perceived during digestion and assimilation; and vipaka is known by ultimate metabolic action.
In sweet substances like ghee, oil, and jaggery, distinct sub-attributes differentiate their therapeutic actions despite sweet primary taste.
Minute variations in elemental composition produce distinct therapeutic results without altering the primary taste classification.
Though primary tastes are classified into six distinct types, minor elemental nuances create subtle pharmacological variations.
Just as general qualities do not exceed their natural class, tastes remain strictly categorized within the primary sixfold division.
Coating of mouth and sweet taste are perceived in ghee and oils, unlike sour pomegranates.
Powerful qualities among heavy and light attributes are designated as Potency (*veerya*), while weaker attributes remain general qualities (*guna*).
Some scholars claim vipaka follows taste strictly into six types, but clinical evidence proves otherwise.
For instance, sweet paddy rice becomes sour upon digestion; non-sour pomegranate does not turn sour; oil turns pungent; and pungent Pippali turns sweet in vipaka.
If post-digestive effect strictly matched taste, such metabolic inversions would never occur in nature.
Barley (*yava*), though sweet and heavy, increases Vata humor; ghee is appetite-kindling and cold; animal fat (*vasa*) is hot yet dampens digestive fire.
Green gram (*mudga*), though pungent in vipaka, pacifies Pitta; black gram (*masha*) increases Pitta; jaggery syrup (*fanita*), though sweet in vipaka, increases Vata.
Such metabolic variations are observed across numerous medicinal and dietary substances.
Sour Amalaki fruit pacifies Kapha and Pitta while increasing Vata; curds (*dahi*) are heavy and kindle digestive fire without worsening Pitta when properly taken.
Kapittha and sour pomegranate are absorbent (*grahi*), whereas sour Amalaki is mild laxative (*sramgana*).
Astringent Dhataki flower is absorbent and cold, whereas astringent Haritaki is laxative and wholesome.
Hence, taste, potency, and vipaka act under the supreme umbrella of the physical substance (*dravya*) itself.
Because specific action (*prabhava*) resides in the substance, the substance (*dravya*) is considered supreme in Ayurvedic pharmacology.
When two drugs have identical taste, potency, and vipaka, yet perform different actions, that distinct action is born of Prabhava (*specific action*).
For example, Danti and Chitraka are identical in taste, potency, and vipaka, yet Danti acts as a purgative while Chitraka does not.
Similarly, raisins (*mridveeka*) match licorice (*madhuka*) in attributes, yet raisins act as laxative; ghee kindles digestive fire unlike milk.
Garlic (*lashuna*), despite pungent taste, hot potency, and unctuous heavy qualities, pacifies Vata and Kapha due to its unique Prabhava.
Red rice (*rakta shali*) pacifies all three humors despite opposing qualities due to its specific action (*prabhava*).
The anti-toxic action of Shirisha and antidote herbs operates entirely through specific action (*prabhava*).
The mysterious pharmacological actions of sacred gems (*mani*), mantras, and divine herbs—drawing out arrows, protective shields, prolonging life—act through Prabhava.
Antidotes that neutralize poisons merely by visual contact or touch operate through specific action (*prabhava*).
Drugs that cause emesis, purgation, or rapid movement of semen act through specific action (*prabhava*).
All these multi-faceted pharmacological phenomena described in scriptures are born of Prabhava, which is inconceivable to raw logic (*achintya*).
Whatever action a drug performs through taste, potency, quality, or vipaka can be instantly transformed by Prabhava; hence Prabhava is beyond ordinary reasoning.
Thus ends the seventeenth chapter (सप्तदशः अध्यायः/seventeenth chapter) named 'Principles of Substances, Qualities, and Tastes' (द्रव्यादिविज्ञानीयः/knowledge of dravya and guna) of Ashtanga Sangraha.



अप्रधानाः पृथक् तस्माद्रसाद्याः संमृतास्तु ते ॥
प्रभावस्तु यतो द्रव्ये द्रव्यं श्रेष्ठमतो मतं ॥
रसादिसाम्ये यत्कर्म विशिष्टं तत्प्रभावजं ॥
दंती रसाद्यैस्तुल्याऽपि चित्रकस्य विरेचनी ॥
मधुकस्य च मृद्वीका घृतं क्षीरस्य दीपनं ॥
कटुपाकरसस्निग्धगुरुत्वैः कफवातजित् ॥
लशुनो वातकफकृघ्नतु तैरेव यद्गुणैः ॥
मिथोविरुद्धान्वातादीन्लोहिताद्या जयंति यत् ॥
कुर्वंति यवकाद्याश्चतत्प्रभावविजृंभितं ॥
शिरीषादि विषादिघ्नं स्वमाद्यं तद्विद्वद्वये ॥
मणिमंत्रौषधीनां च यत्कर्म विविधात्मकं ॥
शल्याकर्षणपुंजनमरक्षायुर्धीवशादिकं ॥
दर्शनाद्यैरपि विषं यन्नियच्छति चागदः ॥
विरेचयति यदुष्णमाशु शुक्रं करोति वा ॥
ऊर्ध्वाधोभागिकं यच्च द्रव्यं यद्वमनादिकं ॥
मात्रादि प्राप्य तत्तच्च यत्प्रपंचेन वर्णितं ॥
तच्च प्रभावजंसर्वमतोऽचिंत्यः स उच्यते ॥
रसेन वीर्येण गुणैश्च कर्म द्रव्यं विपाकेनच यद्विदध्यात् ॥
सद्योऽन्यथा तत्कुरुते प्रभावाद्धेतोरतस्तत्र न गोचरोऽस्ति ॥
॥ इति द्रव्यादिविज्ञानीयो नाम सप्तदशोऽध्यायः ॥ १७ ॥




Now, therefore, we shall explain the chapter (अध्याय/section) on the classification and mechanics of tastes (रसभेद/analysis of tastes) of Ashtanga Sangraha.
Taste (रस/taste) is fundamentally unmanifest in pure water (आप्य/water-based) before elemental combination.
Due to six seasons (षट् ऋतु/six seasons) and varying elemental proportions (महाभूत/primordial elements), water transforms into six distinct tastes (*rasas*).
Sweet taste (*madhura rasa*) originates from the predominance of Earth (पृथ्वी/earth element) and Water (जल/water element).
Sour taste (*amla rasa*) originates from the predominance of Earth (पृथ्वी/earth element) and Fire (तेजस्/fire element).
Salty taste (*lavana rasa*) originates from the predominance of Water (जल/water element) and Fire (तेजस्/fire element).
Bitter taste (*tikta rasa*) originates from the predominance of Air (वायु/wind element) and Space (आकाश/ether element).
Pungent taste (*katu rasa*) originates from the predominance of Air (वायु/wind element) and Fire (तेजस्/fire element).
Astringent taste (*kashaya rasa*) originates from the predominance of Air (वायु/wind element) and Earth (पृथ्वी/earth element).
Sweet taste (*madhura*) coats the mouth, pleases senses, gladdens the body, and is loved by bees and ants (षट्पद पिपीलिका/bees and ants).
Sour taste (*amla*) makes the tongue tremble, burns throat and chest, waters mouth, shrinks eyes, and makes body hair stand on end (रोमाञ्च/goosebumps).
Salty taste (*lavana*) causes salivation in mouth, burns throat and cheeks, and enhances relish for food (अन्न रोचन/appetite relish).
Bitter taste (*tikta*) cleanses the mouth, purifies throat, and temporarily suppresses taste perception (रसन/taste sense).
Pungent taste (*katu*) agitates tongue tip with tingling (चिमिचिमायन/tingling sensation), waters cheeks, and burns mouth, eyes, nose, and body.
Astringent taste (*kashaya*) stiffens the tongue, chokes throat, and causes distress in the heart region (हृदय/heart region).
Sweet taste (*madhura*) nourishes all body tissues (*dhatu*), promotes longevity (*ayushya*), benefits voice, skin, hair, and pacifies Vata, Pitta, burning heat, and poisons.
When consumed in excess (*ati-sevana*), sweet taste causes obesity (स्थौल्या/obesity), sluggish digestion (अग्निसौद/low appetite), heaviness, indigestion (*alasaka*), and excessive sleep.
It also leads to dyspnea (श्वास/asthma), diabetes (प्रमेह/diabetes), goiter (गंडमाला/cervical adenitis), tumors (अर्बुद/tumors), and loss of consciousness.
It produces vomiting (छर्दि/emesis), allergic skin rashes (उदर्द/urticaria), headache, cough (कास/cough), rhinitis (पीनस/catarrh), and intestinal worms (कृमि/parasites).
It brings on elephantiasis (श्लीपद/filaria), fevers (ज्वर/fever), enlarged abdomen (उदर/ascites), and excessive spitting.
Sour taste (*amla*) pacifies Vata wind, aids downward movement (*anulomana*), kindles digestion (*deepana-pachana*), is hot in potency, unctuous, and light.
Excessive sour intake causes body tissue flaccidity (शिथिलता/looseness), Kapha liquefaction, itching (कंडू/itching), anemia (पांडुता/pale anemia), and acute pain.
It provokes erysipelas (विसर्प/erysipelas), bleeding disorders (रक्तपित्त/bleeding disease), excessive thirst, swelling (श्वयथु/edema), and vomiting due to Fire dominance.
Salty taste (*lavana*) breaks stiffness and impaction, kindles appetite (*deepana*), cleanses channels (*srotas*), is unctuous, heating, sharp, and pacifies Vata.
Overuse of salt causes baldness (खलति/hair loss), graying hair (पालित/premature graying), thirst, burning heat, fainting, erysipelas, skin lesions (किटिभ/psoriasis), and gout.
It worsens bleeding disorders (रक्तपित्त/hemorrhage), destroys physical strength (बल/stamina), and depletes vital essence (*ojas*).
Bitter taste (*tikta*) cures anorexia, poisons, worms, fever, burning, thirst, skin diseases (*kushtha*), dries moisture and fat; is light, cold, and digestive.
Excessive bitter intake causes severe loss of tissue strength (धातुबल क्षय/tissue loss), fainting, dizziness (भ्रम/vertigo), and dryness.
It produces roughness (रौक्ष्य/roughness), harshness, and stiff exhaustion across all body systems.
Pungent taste (*katu*) cures indigestion (*alasaka*), swelling, obesity, worms, mouth diseases, skin disorders; dries unctuousness; is light, dry, sharp, and heating.
Excessive pungent intake produces severe thirst (तृष्णा/thirst), intoxication (मद/dizziness), fainting, body exhaustion, and collapse.
It causes tremors (कंप/tremors), vertigo (भ्रम/dizziness), burning exhaustion, and severe emaciation (अतिकर्शन/extreme wasting).
It violently provokes Vata wind in hands, feet, sides, back, and joints with pricking pains.
It induces sharp cutting pains (तोद भेद/cutting agony) due to excessive Fire and Air element dominance.
Astringent taste (*kashaya*) pacifies Kapha, Pitta, and blood; constipates stools, is dry, heavy, cold, and promotes wound healing (*ropana*).
It dries body moisture, restores skin color, and acts as a tissue scraper (*lekhana*).
Excessive astringent intake causes semen obstruction (शुक्ररोध/sperm retention), thirst, abdominal distension (*adhmana*), stiffness, and severe emaciation.
It obstructs body channels (*srotobandha*), causes retention of urine and feces, paralysis (पक्षाघात/hemiplegia), and convulsions (*akshepaka*).
Sweet drug group (*madhura skandha*): Ghee, honey, oil, milk, fat, marrow, sugarcane, raisins, walnut, dates, jackfruit, Shatavari, Bala, Ashwagandha, Gokshura, lotus seeds, Trina-panchamula.
Sour drug group (*amla skandha*): Pomegranate, Amalaki, mango, Matulunga, Tamarind, Karamarda, curds (*dahi*), whey (*mastu*), buttermilk (*takra*), Dhanyamla, and vinegars (*shukta*).
Salty drug group (*lavana skandha*): Rock salt (*saindhava*), sea salt, black salt, alkaline salts (*kshara*), and lead/tin ashes.
Bitter drug group (*tikta skandha*): Aguru, Usheera, Sandalwood, Nimba, Turmeric, Guduchi, Katuki, Kiratatikta, Vacha, Patola, and leafy bitter vegetables.
Pungent drug group (*katu skandha*): Black pepper (*maricha*), Hingu, Vidanga, Garlic (*lashuna*), Onion (*palandu*), Mustard (*sarshapa*), and Panchakola herbs.
Astringent drug group (*kashaya skandha*): Haritaki, Priyangu, Lodhra, Dhataki, Arjun bark, Jamun, Mango seed, Khadira, Lotus, and Red Ochre (*gairika*).
As a rule, sweet foods aggravate Kapha EXCEPT old rice (*purana shali*), barley (*yava*), wheat (*godhuma*), green gram (*mudga*), honey (*madhu*), and wild meat.
As a rule, sour foods aggravate Pitta EXCEPT pomegranate (*dadima*) and Amalaki fruit (*amalaki*).
As a rule, salty foods harm eyesight (*achakshushya*) EXCEPT Saindhava rock salt (*saindhava*).
As a rule, bitter and pungent foods aggravate Vata EXCEPT Guduchi, Patola, fresh ginger (*shunthi*), Pippali, and Garlic (*lashuna*).
As a rule, astringent foods are cold and constipating EXCEPT Haritaki fruit (*haritaki*).
Thus, the primary representative drugs for daily wholesome usage across all six tastes are summarized.
Ghee (Sweet), Amalaki (Sour), Saindhava (Salty), Patola (Bitter), Pippali (Pungent), and Haritaki (Astringent) are supreme in their respective taste groups.
These six prime drugs represent the highest efficacy in their taste classes; next, land habitats (*desha*) of tastes are described.
Marshy land (*aanupa desha*) with abundant water, lotus ponds, lush grass, thick vegetation, heavy animals, and Kapha-Pitta climate is the natural habitat/origin of Sweet taste (*madhura rasa*).
Arid land (*jangala desha*) with dry sand, deep water, sparse vegetation, healthy long-lived inhabitants, and Vata climate is the natural habitat/origin of Pungent taste (*katu rasa*).
Moderate lands combining marshy and arid qualities (*aanupa-jangala sadharana*) produce mixed taste combinations.
Marshy-moderate land gives rise to Salty and Sour tastes, while arid-moderate land gives rise to Bitter and Astringent tastes.
The total mathematical combinations of six tastes across various proportional mixtures equal fifty-seven (*saptapanchashat*).
Explanatory verses on the permutation breakdown of taste combinations follow.
Combining two tastes yields 15 distinct pairs (5 sweet combinations, 4 sour, 3 salty, 2 bitter, 1 pungent).
These fifteen dual-taste combinations (*dvika-samyoga*) provide specific therapeutic actions.
Combining three tastes yields 20 distinct triplets (10 sweet triplets, 6 sour, 3 salty, 1 bitter).
These twenty triple-taste combinations (*trika-samyoga*) serve complex humoral imbalances (*tridosha*).
Combining four tastes yields 15 distinct quadruplets.
Combining five tastes yields 6 distinct quintuplets (each omitting 1 taste).
Four-taste combinations (*chatushka-samyoga*) total 15 distinct variations.
Ten of these quadruplet taste variations include sweet taste as a primary constituent.
Four-taste and five-taste combinations allow precise drug tailoring for multi-humoral diseases.
Omitting one taste at a time from all six gives 6 quintuple taste combinations (*pancha-rasa*).
Including all six tastes together yields 1 sixfold combination (*shad-rasa*), making 63 overall mathematical permutations (*trishashti-bheda*).
Considering primary tastes (*rasa*), secondary aftertastes (*anurasa*), and varying degrees of strength (*tara-tama*), taste combinations become infinite.
These infinite taste variations are skillfully applied by wise physicians according to disease state and drug properties.
Thus ends the eighteenth chapter (अष्टादशः अध्यायः/eighteenth chapter) named 'Classification and Mechanics of Tastes' (रसभेदीयः/mechanics of tastes) of Ashtanga Sangraha.




Now, therefore, we shall explain the chapter (अध्याय/section) on the knowledge of bodily humors, tissues, and wastes (दोषादिविज्ञानीयः/knowledge of doshas and dhatus) of Ashtanga Sangraha.
The human body (देह/body) is rooted in humors (दोष/doshas), tissues (धातु/dhatus), and waste products (मल/waste materials).
Vata humor (वायु/wind humor) sustains the body through respiration (उच्छ्वास निश्वास/inhalation exhalation), enthusiasm (उत्साह/energy), motor activity (प्रस्पंद/movement), sensory acuity (इंद्रिय पाटव/sharp senses), and urging natural discharges (वेग प्रवर्तन/urges urge).
Pitta humor (पित्त/bile humor) sustains the body through digestion (पक्ति/digestion), metabolic heat (ऊष्मा/body heat), appetite (क्षुत्/hunger), thirst (पिपासा/thirst), complexion brilliance (प्रभा/radiance), vision (दर्शन/eyesight), intellect (मेधा/intelligence), courage (शौर्य/valour), and softness (मार्दव/softness).
Kapha humor (श्लेष्मा/phlegm humor) sustains the body through stability (स्थैर्य/steadiness), unctuousness (स्नेह/lubrication), joint binding (संधि बंध/joint stability), virility (वृषता/potency), forbearance (क्षमा/patience), intellect (धी/wisdom), strength (बल/stamina), and greedlessness (अलौल्य/contentment).
Plasma nutrient fluid (रस/plasma tissue) sustains the body through satisfaction (तुष्टि/contentment), nourishment (प्रीणन/refreshing), and feeding blood tissue (रक्त पुष्टि/blood nourishment).
Blood tissue (असरृक्/blood tissue) sustains the body through vitalizing (जीवन/life giving), complexion clarification (वर्ण प्रसाद/skin radiance), and nourishing muscle tissue (मांस पोषण/muscle growth).
Muscle tissue (मांस/muscle tissue) sustains the body through plastering muscles (देह लेप/body plastering), waste secretion, and nourishing fat tissue (मेदः पुष्टि/fat growth).
Fat tissue (मेदः/adipose tissue) sustains the body through lubricating eyes and limbs (नेत्र गात्र स्नेह/eye limb lubrication), firmness of fat, and nourishing bone tissue (अस्थि पुष्टि/bone growth).
Bone tissue (अस्थि/bone tissue) sustains the body through supporting upright posture (देह धारण/body support) and nourishing bone marrow tissue (मज्ज पोषण/marrow growth).
Marrow tissue (मज्जा/marrow tissue) sustains the body through lubrication (स्नेह/unctuousness), strength (बल/stamina), filling bone cavities (अस्थि पूरण/bone cavity filling), and nourishing reproductive tissue (शुक्र पुष्टि/semen growth).
Reproductive tissue (शुक्र/semen tissue) sustains the body through exhilaration (हर्ष/pleasure), physical strength (बल/vital vigor), and embryo production (गर्भ उत्पादन/procreation).
Feces waste (शकृत्/fecal waste) sustains the body through supporting gut stability (अवष्टंभ/intestinal support) and maintaining abdominal air and fire (अनिल अनल धारण/vata fire support).
Urine waste (मूत्र/urinary waste) sustains the body through carrying out water moisture (अम्बु क्लेद निर्वाहण/fluid excretion).
Sweat waste (स्वेद/perspiration waste) sustains the body through holding skin moisture (क्लेद/moisture), skin softness (त्वक् स्नेह/skin unctuousness), and hair health (रोम धारण/body hair support).
Aggravated Vata humor (वृद्ध वायु/increased vata) afflicts the body with emaciation (कार्श्य/lean body), dark discoloration (कार्ष्ण्य/blackish skin), muscle tremors (गात्र कंप/body shaking), twitching (स्फुरण/muscle twitching), craving warmth (उष्ण कामिता/heat desire), loss of consciousness (संज्ञा नाश/fainting), insomnia (निद्रा नाश/sleeplessness), loss of strength and senses (बल इंद्रिय उपघात/sensory weakness), bone pain (अस्थि शूल/bone aching), marrow dryness (मज्जा शोष/marrow drying), constipation (मल संग/stool retention), bloating (आध्मान/flatulence), rumbling (आटोप/intestinal sound), confusion (मोह/delusion), helplessness (दैन्य/misery), fear (भय/anxiety), grief (शोक/sorrow), and delirium (प्रलाप/irrational speech).
Aggravated Pitta humor (वृद्ध पित्त/increased pitta) afflicts the body with yellow skin (पीत त्वक्/yellowish skin), exhaustion (ग्लानि/faintness), sensory weakness (इंद्रिय दौर्बल्य/sensory fatigue), loss of vital essence (ओजो विस्रंस/ojas slipping), desire for coldness (शीत अभिलाष/cold longing), burning sensation (दाह/burning heat), bitter mouth taste (तिक्तास्यता/bitter mouth), excessive thirst (तृष्णा/thirst), fainting (मूर्च्छा/dizziness), reduced sleep (अल्प निद्रता/poor sleep), and anger (क्रोध/irritability).
Aggravated Kapha humor (वृद्ध श्लेष्मा/increased kapha) afflicts the body with coldness (शैत्य/chills), paleness (शौत्य/whitish skin), obesity (स्थौम्य/weight gain), lethargy (आलस्य/sluggishness), heaviness (गौरव/heaviness), limb weakness (अंग साद/body fatigue), channel blockage (स्रोतो पिधान/channel obstruction), fainting (मूर्च्छा/dizziness), sleepiness (निद्रा/excessive sleep), drowsiness (तंद्रा/stupor), dyspnea (श्वास/breathlessness), cough (कास/coughing), salivation (प्रसेक/drooling), nausea (हृल्लास/nausea), loss of digestive fire (अग्नि साद/indigestion), and joint looseness (संधि विश्लेष/loose joints).
Aggravated Rasa plasma (वृद्ध रस/increased plasma) causes excessive salivation (प्रसेक/drooling), anorexia (अरोचक/loss of appetite), perverted taste (आस्य वैरस्य/foul taste), nausea (हृल्लास/nausea), channel blockage (स्रोतो रोध/channel congestion), aversion to sweet taste (स्वादु द्वेष/sweet dislike), bodyache (अंगमर्द/body pain), and other Kapha disorders.
Aggravated Rakta blood (वृद्ध रक्त/increased blood) causes skin diseases (कुष्ठ/skin disorders), erysipelas (विसर्प/red swelling), pustules (पिटिका/skin boils), menorrhagia (असृग्दर/heavy bleeding), eye diseases (अक्षि रोग/eye inflammation), mouth and anal suppuration (मुख गुद पाक/ulcerations), enlarged spleen (प्लीहा/spleen enlargement), abdominal tumor (गुल्म/phantom tumor), abscess (विद्रधि/abscess), hyperpigmentation (व्यंग/freckles), jaundice (कामला/jaundice), loss of digestion (अग्नि नाश/lost appetite), unconsciousness (तमः प्रवेश/blackouts), red skin and eyes (रक्तांग नेत्रि/red eyes), gouty arthritis (*vatarakta*), and other Pitta disorders.
Aggravated Mamsa muscle (वृद्ध मांस/increased muscle) causes goiter (गलगंड/goiter), cervical lymphadenitis (गंडमाला/scrofula), tumors (अर्बुद/fibroids), cysts (ग्रंथि/nodules), palatal and throat disorders (तालु कंठ रोग/throat ailments), heaviness in hips, cheeks, lips, arms, abdomen, thighs, and calves (गौरव वृद्धि/muscle swelling heaviness), and other Kapha-Rakta disorders.
Aggravated Medas fat (वृद्ध मेदः/increased fat) produces pre-monitory signs of diabetes (प्रमेह पूर्वरूप/pre-diabetes symptoms), obesity complications (स्थौल्य उपद्रव/obesity ailments), and other Kapha-Rakta-Mamsa disorders.
Aggravated Asthi bone (वृद्ध अस्थि/increased bone) causes extra bone growth (अध्यस्थि/bony exostosis) and extra teeth (अधिदंत/extra teeth).
Aggravated Majja marrow (वृद्ध मज्जा/increased marrow) causes heaviness in eyes and body (नेत्र अंग गौरव/eye body heaviness), red eyes, and thick-rooted painful ulcers in joint spaces (पर्व ग्रंथि रुक्/painful joint swellings).
Aggravated Shukra semen (वृद्ध शुक्र/increased semen) causes excessive sexual desire (अतिस्त्री कामता/hypersexuality) and seminal calculus (शुक्राश्मरी/semen stones).
Aggravated Purisha feces (वृद्ध शकृत्/increased feces) causes abdominal distension (कुक्षि आधिक्य/enlarged belly), colic pain (शूल/abdominal pain), intestinal rumbling (आटोप/rumbling sound), and heaviness (गौरव/gut heaviness).
Aggravated Mutra urine (वृद्ध मूत्र/increased urine) causes bladder distension (बस्ति आधिक्य/bladder swelling), pricking bladder pain (तोद/stabbing pain), and urinary retention fullness (आध्मान/bladder fullness).
Aggravated Sveda sweat (वृद्ध स्वेद/increased sweat) causes excessive sweating itching (कंडू/skin itch) and foul body odor (दौर्गंध्य/foul smell).
Other increased bodily excretions (मल/wastes) like eye discharge and ear wax cause heaviness (गौरव/heaviness), fluidity (द्रवता/fluidness), and localized itching (कंडू/itching).
Decreased Vata humor (क्षीण वायु/reduced vata) afflicts the body with excessive salivation (प्रसेक/drooling), anorexia (अरुचि/loss of appetite), nausea (हृल्लास/nausea), loss of consciousness (संज्ञा मोह/mental confusion), sparse speech and movements (अल्प वाक् चेष्टा/sluggish activity), loss of joy (प्रहर्ष नाश/lack of joy), limb weakness (अंग साद/body fatigue), and erratic digestion (अग्नि वैषम्य/irregular digestion).
Decreased Pitta humor (क्षीण पित्त/reduced pitta) causes stiffness (स्तंभ/rigidity), coldness (शैत्य/chills), irregular pricking pain (अनियत तोद/variable pain), lack of burning sensation, loss of appetite (अरोचक/indigestion), rough skin (अंग पारुष्य/skin roughness), tremors (कंप/shaking), and whitish nails and eyes (नख नयन शौक्ल्य/pale eyes nails).
Decreased Kapha humor (क्षीण श्लेष्मा/reduced kapha) causes dizziness (भ्रम/vertigo), limb twisting (उद्वेष्टन/cramps), sleeplessness (अनिद्रा/insomnia), bodyache (अंगमर्द/body pain), organ dryness (परिशोष/internal dryness), stabbing pain (तोद/pricking pain), internal burning sensation (दाह/inner heat), cracking joints (संधि स्फोटन/joint popping), tremors (वेपन/shaking), sensation of fumes (धूमायन/smoky feeling), joint looseness (संधि शैथिल्य/lax joints), palpitation (हृदय द्रव/racing heart), and feeling emptiness in Kapha seats (श्लेष्माशय शून्यता/empty chest).
Decreased Rasa plasma (क्षीण रस/reduced plasma) causes intolerance to loud noise (शब्द असहत्व/noise sensitivity), heart palpitations (हृदय द्रव/fluttering heart), tremors (कंप/shaking), dryness (शोष/dry mouth), chest pain (शूल/cardiac pain), sensation of emptiness (शून्यता/emptiness), twitches (स्पंदन/muscle spasms), and exhaustion with thirst upon minor exertion (श्रम तृषा/fatigue thirst).
Decreased Rakta blood (क्षीण रक्त/reduced blood) causes skin roughness (त्वक् रौक्ष्य/dry skin), longing for sour and cold substances (अम्ल शीत अभिलाष/sour cold desire), and vein looseness (शिरा शैथिल्य/collapsed veins).
Decreased Mamsa muscle (क्षीण मांस/reduced muscle) causes wasting of hips, cheeks, and lips (स्फिक् गंड शुष्कता/emaciated cheeks hips), muscle pricking pain (तोद/muscle pain), dry skin (रौक्ष्य/skin dryness), eye fatigue (अक्षि ग्लानि/eye weakness), cracking joints (संधि स्फोटन/cracking joints), and arterial looseness (धमनी शैथिल्य/flaccid arteries).
Decreased Medas fat (क्षीण मेदः/reduced fat) causes spleen enlargement (प्लीहा वृद्धि/enlarged spleen), waist numbness (कटी स्वाप/lower back numbness), feeling empty joints (संधि शून्यता/empty joints), skin dryness (अंग रूक्षता/dry limbs), emaciation (कार्श्य/thinness), fatigue (श्रम/exhaustion), dryness (शोष/body dryness), and intense craving for fatty meat (मेदुर मांस अभिलाष/fatty meat craving).
Decreased Asthi bone (क्षीण अस्थि/reduced bone) causes falling teeth, nails, and body hair (दंत नख रोम शातन/hair nail loss), bone dryness (अस्थि रौक्ष्य/bone dryness), joint looseness (संधि शैथिल्य/lax joints), bone pricking pain (अस्थि तोद/bone pain), and craving for meat attached to bone (अस्थि बद्ध मांस अभिलाष/bone meat craving).
Decreased Majja marrow (क्षीण मज्जा/reduced marrow) causes hollow porous bones (अस्थि सौषिर्य/hollow bones), stabbing bone pain (निस्तोद/deep pain), physical weakness (दौर्बल्य/weakness), dizziness (भ्रम/vertigo), and dark spots before eyes (तमः दर्शन/blacking out).
Decreased Shukra semen (क्षीण शुक्र/reduced semen) causes exhaustion (श्रम/fatigue), weakness (दौर्बल्य/debility), dry mouth (आस्य शोष/dry mouth), blurred vision (तिमिर दर्शन/blurry eyes), pale skin (अंग पांडुता/paleness), weakness (साद/fatigue), impotency (क्लैब्य/erectile dysfunction), testicle pain (मुष्क तोद/testicular pain), smoky feeling, delayed ejaculation (चिरात् सेक/delayed discharge), or ejaculation mixed with blood (सरक्त सेक/bloody semen).
Decreased Purisha feces (क्षीण शकृत्/reduced feces) causes rumbling gas moving sideways and upwards in belly (वायु भ्रमण/trapped gas), intestinal twisting (आंत्र वेष्टन/bowel cramps), flank and heart pain (पार्श्व हृदय पीडा/flank pain), and producing very little stool.
Decreased Mutra urine (क्षीण मूत्र/reduced urine) causes pricking bladder pain (बस्ति निस्तोद/bladder pain), dry mouth (मुख शोष/dry mouth), painful scanty discolored urination (कृच्छ्र अल्प मूत्र/painful scanty urine), or passing bloody urine (सरुधिर मूत्र/hematuria).
Decreased Sveda sweat (क्षीण स्वेद/reduced sweat) causes hair erection stiffness (स्तब्ध रोमता/hair stiffness), hair falling out (रोम च्यवन/hair loss), skin cracking (त्वक् परिपाटन/skin peeling), loss of tactile sensation (स्वाप/skin numbness), skin roughness (पारुष्य/rough skin), and total absence of perspiration (स्वेद नाश/lack of sweat).
Decreased minor excretions (क्षीण मल/reduced minor wastes) cause emptiness (शून्यता/emptiness), lightness (लाघव/lightness), dryness (शोष/dryness), and pricking pain in their respective excretory passages (*malayana*).
Increase and decrease of humors, tissues, and wastes (*vriddhi-kshaya*) should be recognized by the appearance of opposite qualities and clinical symptoms.
Increase and decrease of bodily wastes (*malas*) are recognized by excessive retention (*sangha*) or excessive discharge (*utsarga*).
Depletion of wastes in an increased state afflicts the patient severely due to unaccustomed bodily imbalance (*anauchitya*).
Vata humor resides mainly in bone tissue (*asthi*); Pitta resides in blood (*rakta*) and sweat (*sveda*); Kapha resides in all remaining tissues and wastes.
Therefore, therapies that increase or decrease a tissue also similarly affect its residing humor, except for bone (*asthi*) and Vata (*vayu*) which have inverse relationship.
Almost all bodily tissue increases (*vriddhi*) are caused by over-nourishment (*ati-santarpana*) and are accompanied by Kapha humor.
Conversely, all tissue depletions (*kshaya*) are caused by under-nourishment (*apatarpana*) and are driven by Vata humor.
Therefore, disorders caused by tissue increase or decrease should be treated using depleting (*langhana*) or nourishing (*brimhana*) therapies respectively.
Vata disorders specifically require balanced combination of nourishing (*brimhana*) and depleting (*langhana*) measures.
Everywhere, treatment must utilize substances, qualities, and actions opposite to the cause (*viparita*) or similar in property without conflicting.
Bodily tissues (*dhatus*) grow when a person habitually consumes foods and activities possessing identical qualities (*samana guna*).
Tissues undergo depletion (*kshaya*) when habits possess opposite qualities (*viparita guna*).
For example, heavy heavy tissues in the body are nourished by heavy foods (*guru ahara*) through structural identity.
Light tissues are built by light foods (*laghu ahara*).
Tissues are depleted by foods possessing opposite disintegrating qualities (*viparita guna*).
Thus, blood tissue (*rakta*) is directly replenished by consuming blood; muscle tissue (*mamsa*) by meat; fat tissue (*medas*) by fat; marrow (*majja*) by marrow; semen (*shukra*) by semen; and fetus (*garbha*) by unformed fetal egg.
When identical tissues or foods are unavailable, or impossible to consume due to disgust, rules, or unwholesomeness,
One should consume foods and adopt lifestyle habits that possess a predominance of similar qualities (*samana guna bhuyishtha*).
For instance:
In depletion of semen (*shukra-kshaya*), one should consume milk (*ksheera*), ghee (*ghrita*), and other sweet, unctuous, cold substances.
Physical activities that perform actions identical to a tissue's natural function should be practiced by one desiring its increase.
Similarly, activities opposite in action should be practiced to reduce an excessively increased tissue (*dhatu*).
Furthermore:
Disorders born of increased blood (*rakta-vriddhi*) should be treated with bloodletting (*rakta-mokshana*), blood-cleansing herbs (*prasadana*), and purgation (*virechana*).
Disorders born of increased muscle (*mamsa-vriddhi*) should be treated with purificatory evacuations (*samshodhana*), surgery (*shastra*), alkali (*kshara*), and cautery (*agni-karma*).
Disorders born of increased fat (*medo-vriddhi*) should be treated following obesity-reduction management (*sthaulya-chikitsa*).
Disorders born of depleted plasma (*rasa-kshaya*) should be treated with meat soups (*mamsa-rasa*), wine (*madya*), and milk (*ksheera*).
Disorders born of depleted bone (*asthi-kshaya*) should be treated with medicated enemas (*basti*) and milk-ghee prepared with bitter herbs (*tikta dravya*).
Disorders born of increased feces (*shakrit-vriddhi*) should be treated using anti-diarrheal remedies (*atisara chikitsa*).
Disorders born of depleted feces (*shakrit-kshaya*) should be treated with barley (*yava*), black gram (*masha*), unhusked grain soup, goat meat, sheep meat, and fermented drinks.
Disorders born of increased or decreased urine (*mutra vriddhi kshaya*) should be treated following diabetes (*meha*) and dysuria (*mutrakrichhra*) therapies.
Disorders born of depleted sweat (*sveda-kshaya*) should be treated with oil massage (*abhyanga*), physical exercise (*vyayama*), wine, sleep, warm shelter, and sudation (*svedana*).
Regarding this, the following verses are stated:
Due to sluggishness (*mandagni*) or hyperintensity (*teekshnagni*) of the tissue-digesting fires (*dhatu-agni*), tissues undergo increase (*vriddhi*) or decrease (*kshaya*).
Listen further to this mechanism:
Just as a forest wildfire (*davagni*) grows or diminishes sequentially depending on the fuel it reaches,
In the same manner, the successive chain of bodily tissues (*dhatu parampara*) undergoes increase or depletion.
A substance that is similar in properties increases its corresponding tissue, while a dissimilar substance causes its reduction.
Because from its own metabolic seed (*dhatu beaja*), a corresponding tissue element is quickly produced.
An increased preceding tissue increases the succeeding tissue, while a depleted preceding tissue causes similar depletion in the next.
Vitiated humors (*doshas*) pollute tissues (*dhatus*) through metabolic channels, and both together pollute waste products (*malas*).
Two lower orifices, seven head orifices, and sweat-carrying pores (*sveda-vaha srotas*) are the waste channels (*malayana*).
Waste passages (*malayanas*) harbor corresponding wastes; diseases originating in them will now be described:
Vata-born diseases in these channels will be stated in the chapter on Vata diagnosis (*Vatavyadhi Nidana*).
Pitta humor localized in skin (*tvak*) causes pustular eruptions (*visphota*) and smallpox-like lesions (*masurika*).
Pitta localized in blood (*rakta*) causes erysipelas (*visarpa*) and severe burning heat (*daha*); in muscle (*mamsa*), it causes tissue sloughing (*mamsa-kothana*).
Pitta localized in fat (*medas*) causes burning glandular swellings (*granthi*), excessive sweating (*sveda*), thirst (*trishna*), and severe dizziness (*bhrama*).
Pitta localized in bone (*asthi*) causes intense internal bone burning; in marrow (*majja*), it turns nails and eyes deep yellow (*haridra nakha netra*).
Pitta localized in reproductive tissue (*shukra*) causes foul-smelling yellow-colored semen (*puti pita shukra*).
Pitta in blood vessels (*shira*) causes anger, feverish heat (*tapa*), and delirium (*pralapa*); in ligaments (*snayu*), it causes unquenchable thirst (*trisha*).
Pitta in gastrointestinal tract (*koshtha*) causes intoxication (*mada*), thirst, burning heat, and spreading systemic wasting diseases (*yakhsma*).
Kapha humor localized in skin (*tvak*) causes rigidity (*stambha*) and whitish skin discoloration (*shveta avabhasa*).
Kapha localized in blood (*rakta*) causes anemia (*pandu roga*); in muscle (*mamsa*), it causes tumors (*arbuda*) and cervical lymphadenitis (*apachi*).
It creates a feeling as if the limbs are wrapped in wet leather (*ardra charma avanaddha*) along with profound heaviness (*ati gaurava*).
Kapha localized in fat (*medas*) causes gross obesity (*sthaulya*) and diabetes (*meha*); in bones (*asthi*), it causes bone stiffness (*stabdhatva*).
Kapha in marrow (*majja*) causes pale white eyes (*shukla netra*); in semen (*shukra*), it causes excessive seminal stagnation (*shukra sanchaya*).
Kapha in blood vessels (*shira*) causes channel obstruction (*vibandha*), heaviness, and limb rigidity (*stabdha gatrata*).
Kapha in ligaments (*snayu*) causes joint emptiness (*sandhi shunyata*); in gut (*koshtha*), it causes abdominal enlargement (*jathara unnati*),
Anorexia (*arochaka*), indigestion (*avipaka*), and various other Kapha-born disorders.
Disorders of feces and urine localized in their respective seats will be explained in their respective diagnostic chapters.
Distress and functional damage occur when wastes accumulate in their respective sense organs.
Due to physical overexertion (*vyayama*), hyperintense digestive heat (*ushma taikshnya*), and unwholesome habits (*ahita acharana*),
Vitiated humors (*doshas*) travel from gut (*koshtha*) to peripheral tissues (*shakha*), bones, and vital spots (*marma*) due to the swift mobile nature of Vata wind.
Conversely, humors move back from tissues to gut through channel clearing (*srotomukha vishodhana*),
Driven by humor increase (*vriddhi*), fluid exudation (*abhishyandana*), digestive cooking (*paka*), or by controlling Vata wind (*vayu nigraha*).
Residing in those temporary seats, humors linger waiting for secondary exciting causes (*hetu prateeksha*).
Gaining seasonal or environmental strength (*kala bala*), they become aggravated even in foreign tissue seats (*anyashraya*).
When humors reside in foreign tissue seats, if the host tissue is weak, one should treat the localized humor (*sthani dosha*).
If the invading humor is very powerful and overpowers the host seat, treat the invading humor (*agantu dosha*) primarily.
One should pacify the secondary invading humor (*agantu*) or treat the native host humor (*sthani*) based on relative strength.
The ultimate essence (*tejas*) of all bodily tissues is called Ojas (*ओजः/vital essence*).
Ojas is gentle (*mridu*), watery in nature (*soma atmaka*), pure, clear, reddish-yellow in color (*rakta eeshat peetaka*).
It is the primary essence formed at the time of embryonic conception (*garbha sara*), derived from embryonic nutrient fluid.
As the embryonic heart (*hridaya*) forms in the beginning, Ojas takes up its permanent abode there.
It constitutes the unctuous essence (*sneha*) of all body fluids, wherein fundamental life principles (*prana*) are rooted.
Without Ojas, life cannot exist; nourished by Ojas, all living beings stay refreshed and energized.
Although residing primarily in the heart (*hridaya*), Ojas pervades the entire body and is the supreme seat of life (*jeevitaspada*).
Ojas undergoes depletion (*ojakshaya*) due to anger (*krodha*), hunger (*kshudha*), worry (*dhyana*), grief (*shoka*), and physical overexertion (*shrama*).
In Ojas depletion, the person feels constant fear (*bibheti*), debility (*durbala*), worries continuously, and suffers sensory affliction (*vyathitendriya*).
The person exhibits poor skin luster (*dushchhaya*), mental gloom (*durmana*), dry skin (*rooksha*), and emaciation (*kshama*).
The treatment for Ojas depletion includes vitalizing herbs (*jivaniya aushadha*), milk (*ksheera*), and nourishing meat soups (*mamsa-rasa*).
When Ojas increases (*oja-vriddhi*), the body experiences contentment (*tushti*), nourishment (*pushti*), and enhanced physical strength (*bala*).
Whatever unwholesome food a person feels aversion to, and whatever wholesome food they crave,
By respecting those natural cravings without violating health, one overcomes tissue increase (*vriddhi*) or decrease (*kshaya*).
Agravated or depleted humors (*doshas*) create natural desires for foods that possess opposite or similar qualities.
Both increased and depleted humors indicate their state through cravings, though foolish people fail to recognize them.
According to their strength, increased humors produce their full characteristic symptoms (*roopani*).
Decreased humors abandon their normal functions, while balanced humors (*sama*) perform their natural physiological duties.
The very same humors (*doshas*) that promote growth and vitality when balanced, cause destruction when imbalanced.
Therefore, through wholesome diet and conduct (*hita charya*), one must protect humors from undergo increase or depletion.
Thus ends the nineteenth chapter (एकोनविंशः अध्यायः/nineteenth chapter) named 'Knowledge of Bodily Humors, Tissues, and Wastes' (दोषादिविज्ञानीयः/knowledge of doshas and dhatus) of Ashtanga Sangraha.




Now, therefore, we shall explain the chapter (अध्याय/section) on the differential classification of bodily humors (दोषभेद/subtypes of doshas) of Ashtanga Sangraha.
Vata humor is composed of Space (*Akasha*) and Air (*Vayu*) elements (वायु आकाश/air and space elements).
Pitta humor is composed primarily of Fire (*Agni*) element (तेजः/fire element).
Kapha humor is composed of Water (*Jala*) and Earth (*Prithvi*) elements (जल भूमि/water and earth elements).
All bodily organs and limbs of living beings are nourished by these humors (Vata, Pitta, Kapha) just as plants are nourished by water and nutrients.
Although present throughout the entire body, these humors reside in specific primary locations.
Specially divided into five subtypes each, residing below heart, between heart and navel, and above heart, they sustain the body through mutual cooperation.
The Vata humor is categorized into five distinct subtypes (पंच भेद/five subtypes): Prana, Udana, Samana, Vyana, and Apana.
Prana Vayu (प्राण वायु/vital air) resides in head, chest, throat, nose, tongue, mouth, and navel.
It governs spitting, sneezing, eructation, respiration, and swallowing food (आहार प्रवेश/food intake).
Udana Vayu (उदान वायु/upward air) resides in chest, throat, and nasal passages.
It governs speech production (वाक् प्रवृत्ति/speech), effort, physical energy, strength, complexion, and memory retention (*smriti*).
Samana Vayu (समान वायु/digestive air) resides in GI tract near digestive fire and tissue channels.
It governs receiving food, aiding digestion, separating nutrients from waste, and moving waste downward.
Vyana Vayu (व्यान वायु/pervading air) moves throughout the entire body and governs all physical movements.
It governs walking, extension, flexion, jumping, circulation of nutrient plasma (*rasa samvahana*), and sweat/blood excretion.
Apana Vayu (अपान वायु/downward air) resides in large intestine, rectum, bladder, genitals, testes, navel, and thighs.
It governs the downward expulsion of semen (*shukra*), menstrual fluid (*artava*), urine (*mutra*), feces (*shakrit*), and fetus (*garbha*).
When these five Vata subtypes function normally in their respective locations, they sustain the physical body intact.
Each subtype performs its specific physiological duties to maintain structural and functional health.
When vitiated, these five subtypes abandon normal duties and cause various localized diseases destroying bodily strength.
The Pitta humor is categorized into five distinct subtypes (पंच भेद/five subtypes): Pachaka, Bhrajaka, Ranjaka, Sadhaka, and Tarpaka.
Pachaka Pitta (पाचक पित्त/digestive pitta) resides between stomach and intestines (*amashaya-pakvashaya*).
It digests food, separates nutrients from feces and urine, kindles internal fire, and assists other Pitta subtypes.
Bhrajaka Pitta (भ्राजक पित्त/skin pitta) resides in the skin layer (*tvacha*).
It illuminates skin glow, processes warm poultices, unguents, massages, and absorbs skin applications.
Ranjaka Pitta (रंजक पित्त/coloring pitta) resides in liver (*yakrit*) and spleen (*pleeha*).
It imparts red color to clear nutrient plasma (*rasa*), transforming it into blood tissue (*rakta*).
Sadhaka Pitta (साधक पित्त/intellectual pitta) resides in the heart (*hridaya*).
It governs intellect (*medha*), courage (*shaurya*), wisdom (*dhee*), and fulfillment of cherished desires.
Tarpaka Pitta / Alochaka Pitta (तर्पक/आलोचक पित्त/ocular pitta) resides in eyes.
It enables visual perception and nourishes ocular structures.
The Kapha humor is categorized into five distinct subtypes (पंच भेद/five subtypes): Kledaka, Avalambaka, Bodhaka, Tarpaka, and Shleshaka.
Kledaka Kapha (क्लेदक कफ/moistening kapha) resides in the stomach (*amashaya*).
It moistens dry food mass and nourishes remaining Kapha subtypes throughout the body.
Avalambaka Kapha (अवलंबक कफ/supporting kapha) resides in the chest (*uras*).
It supports shoulder/trika region, heart, and aids other Kapha sites via food essence.
Bodhaka Kapha (बोधक कफ/taste kapha) resides in tongue and throat.
It enables perception of six tastes (*rasa jnana*).
Tarpaka Kapha (तर्पक कफ/nourishing kapha) resides in the head/brain (*shiras*).
It nourishes sensory organs including eyes, ears, and brain tissue.
